| MOD-14 | ✅ | Era weighting | Compare rolling training windows and time-decayed sample weights. **Screened, not resolved (2026-08-19, `docs/era_weighting_screen.md`).** A predeclared six-arm grid (exponential season-decay half-lives {2, 4, 8, 16} vs. rolling windows {6, 10} seasons, all vs. a uniform-weight baseline, `ridge_alpha=10.0` unchanged throughout) ran on two independent instruments: the free 12,500-game CFB XLG-03 benchmark (primary screen) and the real production `weak_stack` recipe on 2,047 non-reserved-season NFL close-graded games (secondary, gated on the CFB lean). Both instruments' self-checks reproduce their frozen baselines bit-for-bit before any candidate arm was trusted. **`half_life_8` (8-season exponential decay) is the strongest arm on every one of six cuts across both instruments** — CFB clean-core week-blocked **+0.347 pts [-0.180, +0.863], P+ 0.8987**; NFL week-blocked **+0.684 pts [-0.542, +1.938], P+ 0.8505**, NFL season-blocked **P+ 0.9533** (lower bound -0.096, one hair from excluding zero) — and never resolves negative on any secondary metric. Two disagreements worth carrying forward rather than smoothing over: `half_life_2` is resolved WORSE on NFL Brier/log-loss (week-blocked Brier -0.0044 [-0.0074, -0.0015], P+0.001) despite a positive accuracy lean, and `rolling_6` is resolved WORSE on CFB Brier/log-loss/margin MAE/RMSE (all four P+ <= 0.0033) — accuracy's coarse ~2-point resolution and the continuous metrics disagree, the same pattern `docs/ridge_alpha.md` found. No arm resolves under the binding crossing-zero taxonomy (every accuracy interval contains zero); all twelve arm x league combinations record `unresolved_below_power` in `registry/weak_signals.json` (`era_weighting_cfb_*`, `era_weighting_nfl_*`). This clears the project's 0.75 CFB screen bar for `half_life_8` but not the 0.90 promotion-claim bar, so no production or rotation-registry change was made here — a future session wanting to promote it needs its own predeclared, opener-graded NFL confirmation window **Completion reconciliation 2026-09-02:** the six-arm rolling/decay grid, CFB and NFL screens, opener read, rotation-assigned confirmation, registry recording, and opt-in append-only `era_weighted_half_life_8` challenger are all implemented. The opener confirmation remains `unresolved_below_power` at -0.2193 accuracy points, 95% [-3.3898,+2.6786], `probability_positive` 0.4246; completing the engineering/research row does not reclassify that result or promote it. | MOD-17 | 🚧 | Unified served numbers: one lattice, one margin, one total | Owner rule 2026-09-05: "if our spread prediction disagrees with the total prediction, we need to understand why we dont have a unified model". Measured that day: the Week 1 card picked KC -3 (production `predicted_margin` +3.19) while the tiebreaker guessed KC 23-20 (margin exactly 3, a push against the pick) because `tiebreaker.py` took its margin from a neighbourhood median instead of the margin model. The unifying object already exists (MOD-05's joint score lattice, `nfl_ats.score_lattice`), so the contract is: every served number -- the pick, the served total, the tiebreaker final, the push and cover probabilities -- is read off ONE lattice centred on the margin model's `predicted_margin` and the totals model's served total; the tiebreaker final is the most probable lattice final on the pick's side of the spread; publishing fails closed on any contradiction. **Still open, the research half:** measure whether a single bivariate residual model (margin residual and total residual fitted jointly on the production feature set, walk-forward) beats the two separate marginal residual models on opener ATS accuracy and total MAE, and report the correlation of the two residuals; predeclare, record through both registries, promote on EV only. **2026-09-05 (research half measured, `docs/mod17_joint_residual_model.md`, `scripts/mod17_joint_residual_screen.py`, `artifacts/mod17_joint_residual/20260905T160219Z/`):** realised margin/total residual correlation +0.0304, 95% [+0.0063, +0.0539] (season-blocked, 17 seasons), `probability_positive` 0.994 -- small, real, and bounds any joint-model gain near r^2≈0.0009 of variance. A predeclared multi-output ridge on the 90-column margin + 24-column totals-wave-2 union (114 cols; mathematically identical to two independent single-target ridges on that union, per a pinned test) scored: margin side (opener sign rule, 1,503 games) **-0.732 accuracy points vs production**, 95% [-2.831, +1.411], `probability_positive` 0.243 -- not promoted. Total side (3,919 games) **+0.00491 mae_improvement** vs the served k=0.1 blend, 95% [-0.00662, +0.01646], `probability_positive` 0.791 -- meets the predeclared EV promotion bar, though out-of-sample R² vs the market is worse with the wider feature set (-0.0762 vs wave-1's -0.0162), so this is a small, shrinkage-heavy win, not a real fit improvement. A second-stage (SUR-lite) model letting each residual see the other's out-of-fold prediction did NOT beat a solo-recalibration control on either target (margin R² -0.0027 coupled vs -0.0008 solo; total -0.0002 coupled vs +0.0002 solo) -- answering the owner's question directly: the two marginal models are not "looking fine" because of hidden compensating errors a joint model would fix; there is too little shared structure between the residuals to exploit. Positive control (leaked margin truth) confirmed the harness's power: +43.979 accuracy points, `probability_positive` 1.0. All three effects recorded `unresolved_below_power` in `registry/weak_signals.json` (`mod17_joint_residual_margin_opener`, `mod17_joint_residual_total_blend`, `mod17_joint_residual_realised_correlation`); no rotation window spent (reused opener archive, disclosed). Status stays 🚧 pending the served-numbers-unification half. **2026-09-05 (production wiring, lane AJ):** wired the research half's decision into the served number. `nfl_ats.served_total` adds two named methods -- `served_total_blend_k01` (today's rule, byte-identical, hash-pinned) and `served_total_joint_residual` (the joint model's own total-column prediction, fit walk-forward on `data/processed/game_features_weak_stack.parquet` with the same (season, week)-block cutoff `nfl_ats.totals_wave2.model_total_view_wave2` already uses, blended at its own measured k=0.1) -- behind an explicit `SERVED_TOTAL_METHOD` constant, now `"joint_residual"` per the EV rule (`probability_positive` 0.791, non-negative point estimate). `nfl_ats.tiebreaker.build_report`/`TiebreakerReport` now compute and persist BOTH numbers every call (`served_total`, `served_total_method`, `comparison_total_blend_k01`, in `tiebreaker.json` and the CLI report), with automatic fall-back to the blend whenever the joint model cannot price a game -- never a hard failure over an optional input. Measured on the live Week 1 card (`nfl-ats tiebreaker --season 2026 --week 1`): served (joint_residual) 43.73 vs comparison (blend_k01) 43.62 (market 43.5, wave-2 totals residual +1.22); the pick-consistent final stayed **KC 24, DEN 20**, strictly on the KC -3 side, identical under both methods. Registered a `totals_served_method` side-ledger prospective challenger (`src/nfl_ats/served_total_challenger.py`, `artifacts/prospective/challengers.json`) that records both served-total candidates for the week's tiebreaker game and backfills the realised total once it is final, wired into `publish-predictions --record-decisions`; `scripts/lockday_rehearsal.py`'s static wiring audit passes (0 errors, 31 active challengers, up from 30). Tests: `tests/test_served_total.py` (16, incl. the blend_k01 hash pin and a walk-forward cutoff leakage guard mirroring `tests/test_totals.py`'s pattern) and `tests/test_served_total_challenger.py` (9), all passing; `tests/test_tiebreaker.py`, `tests/test_publishing.py`'s tiebreaker-specific tests, `tests/test_cli.py`, and `tests/test_lockday_package.py` all still pass against this change (some UNRELATED failures were observed mid-session in `test_publishing.py`/`test_cli.py`/`test_board_terminal.py`/`test_board_improvements.py`/`test_public_board.py`, traced to a concurrent other-lane edit adding `nfl_ats.board_content.verify_number_provenance` to the public-site build path -- not touched by this row, reported separately). Status stays 🚧: the served-total switch is live and tested, but the prospective challenger has zero accrued rows yet and the coordinator has not yet republished the card with the new served total. **2026-09-07 (measured):** the card WAS republished with the new served total -- `tiebreaker.json` for 2026_01_DEN_KC now carries `served_total_method: joint_residual` (served 43.73, comparison blend 43.62; guess KC 24 - DEN 20). Only the prospective rows (first written at the Tuesday 2026-09-08 lock) remain before this row can close. **2026-09-11 (measured, standing audit shipped, `scripts/mod17_served_number_audit.py`, `docs/tiebreaker.md`'s 2026-09-10/11 sections):** the prospective row EXISTS -- `artifacts/prospective/totals_served_method_decisions.parquet` holds one row (2026 week 1, DEN at KC, recorded 2026-09-09T23:13:48Z, blend_k01 43.6115 / joint_residual 43.7238, `realised_total` still NaN until the 2026-09-14 kickoff). What the row's contract had never had was a checkable definition, so nine invariants (C1-C9) were frozen in `docs/tiebreaker.md` BEFORE the audit was first run, and a tenth (C10) added afterwards and disclosed. Run against the live card (active `d49194e04945a5e5`, forecast `margin_predictions/2026-week-01-20260910T210852Z`): **C1, C3, C4, C5, C6, C7 and C9 hold on all sixteen games** -- every printed line matches the served spread, no card probability or displayed confidence falls below 50% on the picked side, every served total is exactly the method total plus the -1 shade, every pick-consistent cell is on the lattice, strictly on the pick side, inside the 3-point hard guard and the 1-point total tolerance. **Thirteen violations in three groups.** (1) **C2/C10, five games: there are three different margins.** `ATL_PIT`, `BAL_IND`, `DEN_KC`, `NO_DET` and `TB_CIN` have `predicted_margin` on the wrong side of their own pick's line. Four are deliberate overlay flips (`coach_fade` on BAL_IND; `pbp08_protection_mismatch_tilt` on the other three) -- a flip mirrors the probability and changes the pick but never moves the margin. The fifth is inside the model: measured by least squares over all sixteen games (max abs error 2.4e-14), the served cover probability is exactly `norm.sf(spread_line - predicted_margin, loc=+0.369057, scale=12.646524)`, so the pick is decided at `predicted_margin + 0.369057` (the `point` field `discrete_push_read.json` already publishes) while the lattice centres on `predicted_margin`, and a third centre exists again in the 50% margin band (`predicted_margin + 1.129237`, asymmetric half-widths, i.e. empirical quantiles rather than `gaussian_median`). The same five games make `nfl-ats tiebreaker` with no published row build its guess for the other side -- rehearsed read-only this session, the CLI guesses KC 23 - DEN 20 "consistent with the KC -2.5 pick" while the card picks DEN +2.5 and its own tiebreaker is KC 22 - DEN 20. NOT fixed here and not narrowed away: re-centring the lattice changes a served number on a rule this row's own text pins to `predicted_margin`, and an overlay flip produces no margin to re-centre on. Bound measured against it: `predicted_margin` is rendered on no reader-facing page (no hit in `board_content.py`, `board_terminal.py`, `public_board.py`, `card_explanation.py`), so the damage today is internal -- `tiebreaker.json`'s `lattice_centre_margin` publishes on the other side of its own `pick_side`. Named next step: a predeclared challenger centring the lattice on the pick-deciding margin. (2) **C8, the published tiebreaker is stale**: `tiebreaker.json` predates the same night's -1 shade wiring -- no `total_low_side_shade_points`, `served_total` 43.7238 where the code now serves 42.7238, score KC 23 - DEN 21 where the code now produces KC 22 - DEN 20. One `nfl-ats publish-predictions` fixes it; handed to the session's publish step because this lane may not rewrite `CURRENT_PREDICTIONS.md`. (3) **Fixed here: the shade was leaking into one arm of a paired ledger.** Because the shade now moves `TiebreakerReport.served_total`, `nfl_ats.served_total_challenger` would have recorded the joint arm shaded (42.7238) against an unshaded blend arm (43.6115) -- a 1.1-point gap of which a full point is the shade -- into a ledger whose one stored row holds the unshaded pair, crediting the shade's known low-side edge to the joint model. `TiebreakerReport.served_total_before_shade` plus one line in the recorder puts both arms back on the method-only scale; measured after the fix, the next recording writes blend_k01 43.6115 / joint_residual 43.7238. The mirror defect in `nfl_ats.tiebreaker_shade_prospective` (its "served" arm now reads an already-shaded published score against a `market_total - 1` challenger, so the two arms are shade-on-blend vs shade-on-market) is guarded rather than redefined: the recorder now skips with a stated reason when the payload's shade is already at or past -1, leaving LEAD-54's owner to decide what that challenger becomes. Gates: `ruff format`, `ruff check`, `mypy src` (224 files) clean; `pytest tests -k "tiebreak or lattice or card or publish" -n 2` 364 passed, 5 skipped, no test edited. Status stays 🚧 on two items: the realised total for the one prospective row (2026-09-14), and the three-margins diagnosis above. **2026-09-11 (the three-margins next step, measured, predeclared in `docs/tiebreaker.md`'s 2026-09-11 section before any number existed):** built the named challenger -- the pick-consistent lattice centred on the pick-deciding margin `point` (`predicted_margin` + the served `gaussian_median` residual location, read from `discrete_push_read.json`, never a new constant) instead of `predicted_margin`, with the overlay-flip branch FROZEN IN WRITING FIRST: when `point` is still on the side the card no longer picks, centre on the line plus the minimum consistent step (`floor(line)+1` for a HOME pick, `ceil(line)-1` for an AWAY pick), rejecting the mirror-across-the-line alternative because it manufactures a conviction the overlay never expressed. **Historical read** (`scripts/mod17_lattice_centre_screen.py`, `artifacts/mod17_lattice_centre/20260911T043520Z/`): 1,537 graded games, 107 week blocks, 2020-2025, off the ACTIVE model's walk-forward opener archive `opener_evaluation/20260910T211255Z`; the per-week probability mapping inverts exactly (worst rebuild gap 1.1e-16), zero hard-guard refusals on any arm. **The challenger removes the C2 defect outright: the centre is strictly on the played side in 100% of games against the served centre's 85.0%, +15.029 accuracy points, 95% [+13.007, +16.939], `probability_positive` 1.0000** -- 231 of 1,537 cards were served with the lattice centred on the side the card does not play, `point` fixes 188 of them and the flip rule the other 43 (the archive's own metadata independently reports 43 opener picks changed by the home-side offset). It is not cosmetic: the two arms print a different final on 747 games (48.6%). **The cost is unmeasurable at this size:** closest-total error -0.02147, 95% [-0.05233, +0.01163], `probability_positive` 0.0985; closest-score error -0.02668, 95% [-0.06153, +0.00912], `probability_positive` 0.0707; on the week's-last-game population (101 games) -0.0198 on both, `probability_positive` 0.3713 / 0.3978. **The predeclared decision rule fires NO** (it required `probability_positive` > 0.5 on closest-total) **and the served card was not touched** -- and the predeclared positive control then showed that metric could never have said yes: centring on the REALISED margin moves closest-total by -0.0221, 95% [-0.0580, +0.0132], `probability_positive` 0.11 (perfect margin knowledge buys no closest-total accuracy, because the total axis is pinned by the served total and the one-point tolerance), while it moves closest-score by +2.371, 95% [+2.149, +2.605], `probability_positive` 1.0000. So the rule stands as written and its NO is reported as its NO; the instrument lesson is that the NEXT predeclaration hangs on closest-score, where the control demonstrably registers. `bounded_by_control` is inadmissible on both error metrics (the control detects 2.4 points, the effect is 0.027) and no interval resolves a sign, so all seven rows are `unresolved_below_power` in `registry/weak_signals.json` under family `lattice_centre_challenger` (`mod17_lattice_centre_closest_total`, `..._closest_score`, `..._pick_consistency`, `..._closest_total_last_game`, `..._closest_score_last_game`, and the two `..._control_*` rows). **Registered as a paired prospective arm** (`src/nfl_ats/lattice_centre_challenger.py`, challenger id `tiebreaker_lattice_centre`, ledger `artifacts/prospective/lattice_centre_decisions.parquet`), wired additively into `publish-predictions --record-decisions` beside the shade and served-total arms, with its `lockday_verify` dedicated-ledger entry and a human display name on the Model Ledger; `scripts/lockday_contract.py` passes (58 active, 42 publish, 0 errors). **Week 1 confirmation (DEN at KC)**, recorder exercised for real with the ledger redirected to a scratch directory so no repository row was written by this lane: the game is one of the four overlay flips, card picks DEN +2.5, served centre +2.7045 (KC's side), `point` +3.0736 (location +0.369057, still KC's side because a flip produces no margin), challenger centre +2.0 by the minimum-step branch; both arms guess **KC 23 - DEN 21** at the currently published 43.7238 total and **KC 22 - DEN 20** at the 42.7238 the code serves now -- identical this week, so nothing a reader sees changes. Gates: `ruff format`, `ruff check`, `mypy src` (225 files) clean; `pytest tests -k "tiebreak or lattice" -n 2` 125 passed, 5 skipped, plus the challenger-wiring tests in `test_cli.py` / `test_board_terminal.py` / `test_lockday_contract.py` / `test_lockday_verify.py`, 12 passed; no test added or edited. | MOD-18 | 🚧 | Spread-size regime: calibrate the model by spread instead of flipping it | Owner, 2026-09-07, on the spread-gap zone fade: "this needs to be addressed holistically. current implementation seems like overfitting." Measured the same day on the active model's opener evaluation (1,503 non-push, 2020-2025): model accuracy by spread bucket 56.2% (0-3) / 56.2% (3.5-6.5) / 52.7% (7-7.5) / **48.5% (7.5-10)** / **44.3% (10.5+)** while its stated confidence sits at 55-57% in every bucket; inside 7.5-10 it takes the FAVOURITE 57% of the time where favourites cover only 45.9% (underdogs 54.1%), and is right 45% on its 111 favourite picks vs 53% on its 83 underdog picks (favourite/underdog labels corrected 2026-09-07 by lane H: nflverse's positive home spread means HOME FAVOURED; the coordinator's first read had the sign reversed); final-margin mass 14.1% on 3, 7.7% on 7, 5.1% on 10, 4.5% on 14. The zone flip's bounds were mined on 2018-2025 (one 2011-2017 replication supports them, P+ 0.91) and the four-rule union it sits in was the best of 127 subsets on the same archive. Program (lane H, predeclared in docs/spread_regime_program.md): (1) diagnosis by era and pick side plus a reliability table; (2) candidates -- C1 signed spread-bucket and key-number features in the ridge, C2 a key-number-aware discrete probability mapping, C3 a walk-forward spread-bucket calibration layer, C4 combinations; (3) the decision read THROUGH the played card, with and without the zone member, so the flip is retired if a principled component absorbs it. Grade at the opener, week-blocked, recorded through both registries. Done when the served probability is calibrated by spread size and the zone flip is either retired or kept on a measured composed-card marginal. **Owner, later the same day: "i said it before and it seems like it was rejected/ignored - the football scores dont follow a smooth gaussian.... its a multi-modal distribution."** Read: MOD-05 (2026-08-17) confirmed exactly that (dip p = 0.000; 14.6% of games on 3 vs 5.3% normal) and set it aside for ATS because the POOLED residual tests smooth -- a composition fallacy for a per-line cover probability; MOD-08's Gaussian-vs-ECDF promotion compared two pooled-residual reads and never tested a multimodal margin model. Now binding in AGENTS.md ("Football margins are multimodal, not Gaussian"). Consequence for this row: **C2 (the discrete, key-number-aware cover probability conditional on the line -- the MOD-05 lattice recentred on the prediction, or an equivalent integer-mass mapping) is the PRIMARY direction; C1/C3 are supplements; the Gaussian read becomes a challenger.** | 2026-09-07: opened; lane H running with C1-C4; C2 elevated to primary after the owner's restatement. **Owner, AFK order, same day: "no more arbitrary pick flips because we can't explain a drop in accuracy at certain point thresholds... we need to be able to explain these things so we can understand the weak points in the model."** Acting on it: lane I retires the spread-gap zone fade from the PLAYED card (three mechanism members remain: coach fade, division revenge, player arrests; measured cost on the mined archive 55.42% -> 55.22%, an in-sample selected number; the four-member union keeps accruing as a former-production challenger and the zone fade stays a challenger); lane J puts a "Where the model is weak" table (accuracy, pick side and stated confidence by spread bucket, from the active model's own opener evaluation) on the Model page and in the assistant. Rule now binding in AGENTS.md ("No unexplained threshold flips on the played card"). **Lane H result (Codex; coordinator re-ran its 223 tests and read laneH/results.json; lane H's claim that it recorded its arms was FALSE -- zero registry entries existed, the coordinator recorded all five as `mod18_spread_regime_opener_v1_*`):** every arm leans below the incumbent at the opener on 1,503 non-push games -- C1 spread-regime ridge features -1.73 pts [-3.45, +0.00] P+ 0.02; C2 rounded pooled-residual mapping -0.60 [-1.80, +0.59] P+ 0.15; C3 walk-forward bucket calibration -2.26 [-5.63, +1.13] P+ 0.09; C1+C2 -1.66 P+ 0.03; C1+C3 -3.33 P+ 0.03. Through the frozen four-member card C2 55.29% vs 55.42%; C2 changes 0 of 16 Week 1 sides. **Lane H's C2 was a rounded POOLED-residual read, by its own admission "not a completed conditional key-number model" -- the owner's actual proposal (a discrete margin distribution conditional on the line) is still untested and is the next lane.** Diagnosis by era (nflverse-close grade before 2020): the 7.5-10 dip replicates in 2011-2017 (45.8%, n=238; favourites cover 47.5%) and is muted in 2018-2019 (50.8%, n=61); C1/C3's walk-forward failure says the effect is not learnable as a stable regime shift, which is consistent with the owner's overfitting read. **Label correction:** lane H found the coordinator's favourite/underdog columns reversed (nflverse positive home spread = HOME favoured; verified: mean home result +5.85 on 908 such games) -- corrected in this row, the queued-lead note, UI-20 and lane J's module (`model_weak_spots.py` had inherited the reversed sign; fixed and re-tested before publication). **Lane I result (Codex; coordinator applied the three out-of-scope guard patches it flagged -- `pick_refresh` policy-id guard, `clv` OR-union validator, `signal_ledger` reader copy -- and re-ran: 123 + 533 tests green, rehearsal 41 active paths / 0 errors):** the played card is now the THREE-member union coach fade + division revenge + player arrests, policy id `overlay_union_coach_division_revenge_player_arrests_v2`; the spread-gap zone member is out of the played composition, the flip-line scan, the card explanations and the crew-tilt flip map; the retired four-member card keeps accruing 2026 evidence as challenger `overlay_four_member_union_retired_20260907` (recorder wired into `publish-predictions --record-decisions`, fail-open), and the standalone zone challenger is untouched. Isolated chain proof: Week 1 changes exactly one side, **CLE +7.5 -> JAX -7.5**; scratch headline 55.2% (three-member row 0.5522; retired four-member 0.5542 kept as the comparison reference); number provenance passes. docs/spread_gap_zone_retired.md. Live activation via the coordinator's real refresh follows. **Live (measured 2026-09-07 12:44 ET):** the real refresh activated it -- forecast 20260907T164352Z, card JAX -7.5, headline 55.2%, no rule label on the board. **Lane K (Codex; the owner's actual proposal, verified: 21 registry entries under `mod18_conditional_margin_v1_*`, 106 tests, gates clean):** a DISCRETE integer-margin cover probability conditional on the line, three predeclared forms -- K1 a 2.5-point kernel over prior predicted margins with integer atoms kept, K2 the empirical margin lattice translated so its median matches the prediction, K3 K1 conditioned additionally on which side of the nearest key number the line sits -- opener-graded vs the active model on 1,503 non-push games: **K1 -1.80 pts [-4.64, +1.07] P+ 0.105 (379 picks change); K2 -0.27 [-1.25, +0.73] P+ 0.27; K3 -2.86 [-6.31, +0.60] P+ 0.05**; Brier/log loss also lean worse (K2's Brier interval wholly below zero); through the three-member card K2 -0.20 P+ 0.28. **The instructive part:** K1 fixes exactly the band the owner asked about -- 7.5-10 goes from 48.5% to 55.2% right, and its stated confidence there rises to 59% -- but it loses on 0-3 (54.5% vs 56.2%) and 3.5-6.5 (50.2% vs 56.2%), where the smooth read was already calibrated; the key-number structure matters where the line sits near a mass point and adds noise where it does not. K2's translation displaces the absolute key numbers (push rate at |line| = 3 predicted 4.1% vs 10.1% realised) -- MOD-05's recentring constraint confirmed. Week 1: K2 changes 0 sides, K1 6, K3 4. Decision: keep the incumbent mapping; nothing closed (all `unresolved_below_power`); the next step is a HYBRID that is not a threshold flip -- a conditional read whose weight on the key-number lattice is itself learned from prior games as a function of the line's distance to the nearest key number, so it defers to the smooth read away from 3/7/10/14 and to the lattice near them. **Lane L, hybrid margin mapping (Codex, 2026-09-07 evening; coordinator read docs/hybrid_margin_mapping.md and measured 136 registry entries under family `mod18_hybrid_margin_v1`, 58 live comparison cells + 78 invalidated diagnostic mis-recordings):** the learned hybrid -- cover probability = w(d)*lattice + (1-w(d))*smooth with w(d) = w0*exp(-d/tau) fitted walk-forward on prior non-push Brier, d = distance of |line| to {3,7,10,14} -- H1 scores -0.60 pts [-2.06, +0.86] P+ 0.196 at the opener on 1,503 non-push games and H2 (w also by spread size) -0.80 [-2.62, +1.00] P+ 0.180; through the three-member card -0.33 P+ 0.293 and -0.20 P+ 0.381; H1's Brier/log loss improve by +0.00005/+0.00017 (P+ 0.54/0.55), H2's worsen. Both change three Week 1 model sides (CLE->JAX already on the card, DAL, NO). Incumbent kept; every cell `unresolved_below_power`. **Diagnosis D is the payoff:** the 7.5-10 weakness is a probability-shape problem concentrated in HOME UNDERDOG games -- realized home cover minus the smooth probability is +14.5 pp [+3.0, +26.3] P+ 0.993 on the 69 home-underdog games there (K1's lattice gap -0.4), while the 125 home-favourite games show no gap (-0.1 pp); and at 10.5+ the POINT forecast itself is off: actual home margin exceeds it by +2.91 [+0.83, +5.01] P+ 0.997 (134 games), +5.18 [+2.10, +8.20] P+ 0.999 on the 65 games where the model took the underdog (that stratum is by PICK SIDE, not home/away -- an earlier draft of this sentence misread it as home underdogs; lane P's true home/away split from the same evaluation: 10.5+ home underdogs 32 games covered 59.4% vs 47.0% expected, 10.5+ home favourites 99 games covered 57.6% vs 49.4% with the model right only 39.4%). So the model under-locates the HOME team on big spreads on both sides, and specifically home underdogs at 7.5-10: a named mechanism rather than a threshold. Next lanes (launched 2026-09-07 evening): lane Q predeclares `mod18_home_dog_location_v1` (a home-underdog-points feature and a hinge above 7 so the ridge learns the correction walk-forward, graded at the opener through the played card; a home-side spread-size term was proposed as a third arm once lane P's split arrived, allowed only if lane Q had not yet frozen). **Lane Q result (Fable; coordinator measured 88 registry entries under `mod18_home_dog_location_v1` and re-ran its 77 tests):** baseline replayed the archive exactly, then Q1 `home_dog_points` -0.33 pts [-1.06, +0.39] P+ 0.155 at the opener (27 picks change) and Q2 with the hinge above 7 -0.27 [-1.26, +0.73] P+ 0.273 (62 change); through the three-member card 0.00 [-0.54, +0.54] P+ 0.462 and -0.27 P+ 0.235; Brier/log loss within 0.0001 of the incumbent; windows 2020-21 +0.22 / 2022-23 -1.36 [-2.71, -0.19] / 2024-25 +0.19 for Q1. Incumbent kept on expected value; nothing closed; three opener windows spent unresolved. The diagnosis is the payoff again: the home point error does NOT shrink (10.5+ home underdogs +4.50 -> +4.40 / +3.04 on 32 games) because the ridge weights the term under 0.1 pt/pt with a sign that flips across training epochs, and on the TRUE home split 10.5+ home favourites are also under-located, +2.41 [+0.11, +4.68] P+ 0.98 (102 games). Q3 = |spread| was correctly not added (an exact linear combination of `spread_line` and Q1). Week 1: Q1 changes 0 sides, Q2 would flip NE +3.5 at SEA (p 0.501), not played. Reader text on a touched game: "the line sits right on 7, a number games land on a lot, so this pick is read off how games with lines like this actually finished". Codex lane AC reviewed lanes W/AB/Z before the lock; three of its four findings fixed in the tree the lock runs on: the era-weighted recorder's reproduction check now compares the number the card refit returned (it would have refused every card with a key-line pick); candidate arms in the own-arm challengers no longer inherit the served pick override (their own probability is what gets recorded); `tuesday_opener_quotes` restricted to the game's own Tuesday, pregame (an earlier week's post-lock quote had been standing in). The fourth -- the Wednesday refresh mixing a discrete pick probability with the model's smooth three-way split -- was fixed the same hour by Fable lane AE (patch applied before the lock; 133 refresh/key-line/discrete tests + the full suite green): the late-week refresh now rebuilds the discrete three-way split at the frozen Tuesday line before applying the key-line pick read, exactly in margin-predict's order, restores both from the sidecars on the fallback path, and leaves a pre-promotion card byte-identical; the atom test is declared as keyed to the frozen Tuesday line. Left as is, disclosed: five pre-existing registry rows (mod17 joint residual, movement-rule chain, two Best Pick composed rows, an NFL.com refresh row) carry "raw model" in their recorded text. **Own-arm refit challengers aligned the same morning (Codex lane B, gpt-6-astra low effort, read-only patch; coordinator applied it minus the Best Pick nomination hunk -- that refit is a served decision and stays as measured -- fixed its fixture and measured 111 tests green):** new `nfl_ats.card_refit.load_card_refit` replays the card's recorded offset (metadata first, sidecar second, never a refit) and the card's recorded probability mapping in the incumbent arms of expected lineup loss, trade-deadline drag, the QB-revenge stack and the era-weighted half-life overlay; a card without the promotion keeps the historical refit bit-for-bit, with the warning carried in the recorder result. `inactives_refresh` was already aligned through `plan_refresh`; `pool-card-at-lines` is a standalone command and stays independent. **Lane T result (Fable, concurrent with S; coordinator measured 90 registry rows under `mod18_home_side_mapping_v1` and re-ran its tests):** the probability SHAPE conditioned on the home side, two frozen arms on the unchanged point -- T1 a home-side x spread-band centre shift of the smooth read (shrunken prior-game residual means, 100-game prior) -1.46 pts [-3.27, +0.33] P+ 0.053 at the opener, -1.40 P+ 0.028 through the card; T2 lane K's integer-margin lattice conditioned on the home side -2.59 [-5.65, +0.54] P+ 0.049, -1.26 P+ 0.148 through the card; Brier/log loss worse for both. The diagnostic answer: T2 removes the 7.5-10 home-underdog gap entirely (+14.5 pp -> -0.2 pp, right 46% -> 58%) but pays at 0-3 home favourites (-0.2 -> -5.4 pp) and 3.5-6.5 home underdogs (+1.6 -> -4.5 pp), the same trade K1 made; T1 barely reaches the gap (about 46 trailing rows against the 100-game prior). Push rate at |line| 3 holds (8.8% vs 10.1%). Incumbent mapping kept; nothing closed; three windows spent unresolved. Together with lane S this says the big-spread weakness is a LOCATION problem the mapping cannot fix without breaking the small-spread bands, which is why S2 (location, mapping unchanged) is the arm that gets played.; lane P puts the home/away split on the Model page's weak-spots table. **C2 is now the live candidate and it is OPEN, not tried: price the served cover probability off the discrete conditional distribution at EVERY half-point line, not only where the line equals an atom** (predeclared with its grading in `docs/key_line_pick_read.md`, "TODO, predeclared"). Why it moved up (measured 2026-09-08 on 4,431 completed regular-season games, `data/processed/game_features.parquet` 2009-2025): the owner's pool posts every line as a half point, nine of this week's sixteen within half a point of 3, and against a fitted normal the empirical cover rate is -2.14 pts at 2.5, +2.95 at 3.5, +2.66 at 6.5, +4.49 at 7.5, +3.69 at 10.5 -- opposite signs either side of the 3 atom, understating the 7.81-point cliff across it by 2.87x, because the 14.58% of finals landing exactly on |3| fall wholly on ONE side of a half-point line instead of pushing. The narrow exact-match atom read (lane T, served) therefore cannot fire on the pool's lines, which is recorded as `status: inapplicable` in its sidecar and is NOT a finding against the discrete read: `serve_discrete_three_way` still serves every game and every lane K/S/T cell stays `unresolved_below_power`. **Lane C3 (Opus, 2026-09-10; `docs/spread_size_calibration.md`, `scripts/spread_size_calibration.py`, 240 registry cells `spread_size_calibration_v1_*`, all `unresolved_below_power`): the owner's "calibrate by spread instead of flipping it" done as a CALIBRATION, fitted on 2020-2022 and scored on 2023-2025.** The diagnosis first: it reproduces on the CURRENT active model (`d49194e04945a5e5`, opener evaluation `20260910T211255Z`, 1,503 non-push) -- raw accuracy by bucket 56.2 / 56.2 / 52.7 / **48.5** / **44.3** with stated confidence flat at 55.4-56.6%, the 2026-09-07 numbers to the decimal on a model refitted since. As SERVED (S3) the LOCATION fix has taken 7.5-10 to 51.5% and 10.5+ to 47.3% while the stated confidence moved a tenth of a point, so the CONFIDENCE has never been corrected: a reader on a 10.5+ game is told 55.9% about a pick that lands 47.3%. Nine arms of `logit(p) -> a_b + s_b*logit(p)` per spread bucket (100-game shrinkage to the identity), on lane K's discrete mass-preserving lattice and -- as the challenger AGENTS.md allows -- on the smooth read, graded at the opener on 799 held-out games: the lattice with the correction (A1) -2.00 pts [-5.03, +1.01] `probability_positive` 0.100 vs S3 and -1.88 P+ 0.089 vs the played KL1b, Brier +0.00056 P+ 0.641; the smooth read with the same correction (A3) -1.63 P+ 0.145, Brier +0.00049 P+ 0.636. **The decomposition is the finding:** against the SAME lattice with no correction, the spread-conditional recalibration is worth **+0.876 pts [-0.625, +2.387] P+ 0.871 and +0.00114 Brier P+ 0.895** -- the calibration helps, and it is the LATTICE SIDE READ that loses (A0 alone -2.88 vs S3). Per bucket A1 wins exactly where the owner pointed (10.5+ +5.17 P+ 0.817 with the family's best Brier gain +0.00865 P+ 0.859; lines quoted at 7 +10.81 P+ 0.902 on 37 games) and repays all of it in 3.5-6.5 (-5.86 [-10.47, -1.12] P+ 0.007), where the smooth read is already the best-calibrated thing on the board -- the K1/M1/T2 trade again, now with a chronological fit/score split. The fitted tables name a mechanism rather than a threshold: the intercept runs positive in the big buckets (+0.129 at 10.5+ after S3 has taken its share) and the slope falls away with spread size (0.91 / 0.61 / 0.87 / 0.36 / 0.05, the 7 bucket the exception and the thinnest cell at 37 games), i.e. the model's own confidence ordering carries little information on big spreads and pointed the wrong way at 10.5+ on the fit seasons. **Proposal, not served: A8** -- the scale-only correction fitted on and applied to the read that is PLAYED, with the slope floored at 0.05 so it can never invert a side: **0 of 799 picks changed, accuracy identical (54.944%), Brier +0.00065 [-0.00173, +0.00309] P+ 0.706**, and the held-out 10.5+ stated-minus-realised gap goes -5.1 -> +1.4 (50.3% stated against 51.7% realised); it understates by about five points at 3.5-6.5 and 7.5-10, where the fit seasons put a weak slope on both (0.53 and 0.16). A7 is the same correction on the smooth read (+0.00060 P+ 0.691) and is reported only for comparison. A5-A8 are post-hoc: A0-A4 were the frozen set, and the pick-preserving arms were added after they were scored. Week 1 read-only (nothing regenerated): A1 would change 4 of 16 sides (BUF at HOU, CHI at CAR, NO at DET, TB at CIN, all near coin flips), A7 and A8 zero by construction. Incumbent kept for the PICK on expected value; nothing closed; 15 of 240 cells have a whole interval on one side of zero against roughly 12 expected on nested correlated arms, and the 3.5-6.5 lattice loss meets the letter of `wrong_sign_resolved` for the direction "the lattice beats the smooth read at 3.5-6.5" but is left `unresolved_below_power` for a human look (lane G/U precedent). Open follow-up: wire A8 as one displayed-confidence policy shared by the Model page's weak-spots table and the card, and carry the spread-conditional correction into whichever lane gets the lattice side read right on half-point lines. **Serving lane, 2026-09-10 (docs/spread_size_calibration.md, "What happened when the serving lane tried to wire A8"): A8 was NOT served, because the premise it was scored against had already moved.** Lane AH shipped a displayed-confidence transform hours earlier, so the Decision score is no longer the raw stated probability; measured on the active model's own archive (1,503 non-push, walk-forward for every arm, week-blocked paired bootstrap 20,000 draws seed 20260821) A8 in place of that incumbent is **Brier -0.00049 P+ 0.312 and log loss -0.00625 P+ 0.150**, A8's scale stacked on top is 0.316 / 0.224, and the incumbent stacked on top of A8 is 0.531 / 0.399. Replacing it is taking the 31/69 and the 15/85 side, so the incumbent was kept -- the decision is expected value, and here it points at the incumbent. **What WAS served is the one part of A8 that wins: its never-invert guard**, the property that its floored scale can never carry a probability across 0.5. The incumbent had no such property and printed a score below 50% on **179 of 1,503** picks the card would have made; flooring it is **+0.00030 Brier and +0.00060 log loss, both P+ 0.912**, and takes that count to 0 of 1,503. Disclosed against it: the floor fires in the buckets the owner named, so the bucket-average overstatement widens there (7.5-10 -0.89 -> -1.70, 10.5+ -3.73 -> -4.77, lines at 7 -3.64 -> -4.28) -- a coarse bucket average traded against the two proper scoring rules for the per-game number a reader acts on, both of which favour the floor. Shipped as `displayed_confidence_reliability_v2_pick_side_floor`, plus two guards the brief asked for: the display is fitted **fail-closed** on the opener evaluation matched to the ACTIVE model (a missing or mismatched match now raises instead of falling back to the newest archive on disk), and `attach_displayed_confidence` asserts at runtime that no displayed score lands below 50% on a side the card picks. The Model page's weak-spots table carries a plain-English note saying the stated-confidence column is the model's own number while the score beside each pick is the corrected one, and that it is never shown below 50% on a side being picked. Rehearsed through `nfl-ats publish-predictions`: **0 of 16 Week 1 scores floored, 0 sides changed**, card diff the footnote sentence and the publish stamp only; `nfl-ats publish-board` regenerated the four pages (isolated note contribution +957 bytes model.html, +476 index.html, history and findings byte-identical). Still open: the lattice side read on half-point lines, which is what A8's parent correction is actually waiting on. **2026-09-11, lane P1 (`docs/mod18_pool_shaped_read.md`, family `mod18_pool_shaped_read_v1`; numbers reported by the lane, registry rows counted by the coordinator):** the half-point side read is answered and the premise behind the question was wrong. The archive's decision line is the captured Tuesday-opener consensus, not nflverse `spread_line`, and 842 of its 1,537 games (54.78%) are already exactly a half point — the pool-shaped archive already existed and had never been sliced out. Graded on those 842 games every arm loses through the played card: G2≡G4 -0.594 [-2.334, +1.066] P+ 0.248, G1 -0.713 P+ 0.273, G3 -0.831 P+ 0.199. Structural invariants verified: KL1b touches 0 of 842 pool games and is bit-identical to S3 there; G2≡G4 on POOL. The mechanism does not cross the half point — lane T's +8.571 at exactly 7 becomes -3.846 [-10.417, +2.454] P+ 0.117 on 6.5/7.5 — and the family's entire measured value is the rule already served (G2 vs KL1b off-pool +0.000, 0 flips). Positive control recovered its injected +1.0 as +0.950 [+0.360, +1.620] at SE 0.319 against arm SEs of 0.87-1.21, so it bounds nothing; all 234 cells `unresolved_below_power`. No arm above P+ 0.5, so no challenger registered (declining at P+ 0.20-0.27 is the EV side). Week 1: 16/16 lines half points, KL1b touches 0. Consequence: `key_line_pick_read_off_incumbent` cannot accrue evidence while every pool line is a half point. Next: grade the family at a single book's posted half point from `data/market/raw/*/quotes.parquet` at `tue_open`, which needs an opener evaluation refitted at that line. **Unpredeclared, flagged, not acted on (own family `mod18_overlay_union_by_line_shape_v1`):** the played three-member overlay union reads -1.425 [-3.933, +1.160] P+ 0.136 on half-point lines and +4.841 [+1.506, +8.235] P+ 0.999 off them — its whole measured contribution sits on lines the pool never posts. A predeclared mechanism lane (era/book-count composition of the whole-number subset, push handling, per-season split) was opened the same day; no card change until it names a mechanism. **2026-09-11, lane Q1 — mechanism for the line-shape split (`docs/overlay_union_line_shape.md`, predeclared before measurement; 60 rows in family `overlay_union_line_shape_mechanism_v1`, all `unresolved_below_power`; numbers reported by the lane):** none of composition, push handling or spread magnitude accounts for any of the +5.891-point gap (season × book-count standardisation leaves +5.956, decided-by-a-point grading +6.174, line-size standardisation +6.841). Direction and base rate contribute 23%: the model's own pick is -4.538 [-9.641, +0.500] P+ 0.039 on whole-number lines relative to half-point ones, and against a same-size, same-direction random flip the union is positive on both shapes (+0.763 half, +5.285 whole). The remainder is located in one member — division revenge carries 78% of the split (whole archive +0.732 P+ 0.834; half-point lines alone -1.425 [-3.147, +0.241] P+ 0.049, 78 flips) — but not explained. Both season halves show the gap positive; no closing ground. **Verdict: unexplained threshold, published as a diagnosis; it may not gate overlay membership.** The union stays on the card (+1.331 [-0.727, +3.393] P+ 0.898 on 1,503 games). Successor question is a model diagnosis: why the model is 4.5 accuracy points weaker on whole-number opener lines. **2026-09-11, lane Q2 (`docs/whole_number_line_model_diagnosis.md`, family `whole_number_line_model_diagnosis_v1`, 40 rows; numbers reported by the lane):** the model's 4.5-point weakness on whole-number Tuesday openers is diagnosed and it is NOT the discrete mapping. On the active model `7786467eabe418b8` (evaluation `20260911T161354Z`) the gap is -4.538 [-9.612, +0.484] P+ 0.040 (56.53% on 842 half-point openers, 51.99% on 627 graded whole-number ones). Whole lines exactly on 3 or 7 are the strongest part of that domain (+2.355 [-6.011, +10.488] P+ 0.712) and removing the key numbers makes the gap larger (-6.395); push handling leaves it at -4.25 to -4.79 — the atom's treatment is exonerated. Located instead: (1) the whole-number opener is an away-biased price (home beat it by +0.671 points on average vs -0.031 on half points, and the model leans 0.226 the other way); (2) the model's edge is a book-disagreement edge — 57.96% where the captured books disagreed vs 51.79% where they were unanimous (+6.165 [-0.789, +13.340] P+ 0.958), and the shape gap nearly vanishes among unanimous openers (-1.795 [-13.587, +9.714]); (3) stated confidence is flat across shapes (55.85% vs 55.58%) against 56.53% vs 51.99% realised. Composition, line size, model edge, the home-side offset (which reduces the gap by 1.04) and base rate account for none; 39% of the gap is present in the market's own movement signal and 62% survives regrading at the close. Reliability positive (odd/even-week Pearson +0.692), so nothing closes. Fix direction: grade and fit against a single book's posted half-point line (lane P2 running the same day) and give the served confidence something to vary with. No flip on line shape may touch the card: the pool posts only half points, so every served pick already sits on the stronger shape; the consequence is for the archive-graded headline, which blends two populations of different difficulty. **2026-09-11, lane P2 (`docs/single_book_opener_grade.md`; numbers reported by the lane):** the family was regraded at a line a book actually posted. `nfl-ats opener-evaluation` now takes `--opener-line-source` and `nfl-ats opener-line-series` builds the series from the per-book Tuesday-opener archive; line-source runs land in `artifacts/opener_evaluation_line_source/` and never claim to be the active model's evaluation. Book chosen by coverage before grading: DraftKings (1,597 of 2020-2025 games; a single book posts a whole number on 43% of openers, FanDuel 25%). Headline: the active model scores 53.83% at DraftKings' posted opener vs 54.50% at the cross-book consensus on the same 1,477 paired games (-0.677 points [-1.279, -0.134], P+ 0.009) — the published history is graded on a median of prices and the number a pool settles is about seven tenths lower. Only 20 of 1,521 picks change side; the served home-side offset is worth +0.672 [-0.199, +1.547] P+ 0.939 at the consensus and +0.201 [-0.754, +1.188] P+ 0.657 at a posted book line — the modelling defect this surfaced. KL1b fires on 0 of the 864 half-point DraftKings games; G2 on the 485 games at 2.5/3.5/6.5/7.5 reads -1.443 [-4.366, +1.452] P+ 0.166 through the card, so no challenger registered. 76 cells recorded under `single_book_opener_grade_v1` / `_posthoc_v1`, all `unresolved_below_power`. Open next: refit the home-side offset at a posted half-point line; build a pool-line series from the intraday archive; grade FanDuel. UI-20 candidate: a Model-page number for 'how the model scores against one book's posted opener vs the average across books', sourced from the line-source artifact. |

## Phase 7 — simulations

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| SIM-01 | ✅ | Margin Monte Carlo | **Implemented 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/margin_simulation.py`, `docs/margin_monte_carlo.md`): deterministic residual resampling from a fitted margin model, with latent and integer-settled draws retained for audit; repository-compatible two-way and three-way ATS probabilities; integer-line push handling; and a fail-closed guard requiring every target game to occur strictly after the model training cutoff. This is simulation plumbing only—no experiment was scored and no wagering action exists. |
| SIM-02 | ✅ | Drive-level simulator | **Implemented 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/drive_simulation.py`, `docs/drive_simulator.md`): deterministic possession simulation with field position, drive outcomes, pace, score-aware behavior, integer scoring, and auditable drive/game output. This is scenario plumbing only—no experiment was scored and no wagering action exists. |
| SIM-03 | ✅ | Player availability scenarios | Mixture of lineups propagated into margin distributions. **2026-09-05 CX21:** measured (`artifacts/experiments/lineup_mixture/results.json`) K=200 centered LEAD-62 mixture on 1,236 opener games/89 weeks (2020–2024; 2025 panel absent): Brier improvement +0.00003251, probability_positive 0.859; accuracy +0.000 points, probability_positive 0.34535; 2 historical flips cancel, 0/16 Week 1 base picks change; six main/control accuracy/Brier records are unresolved_below_power; see `docs/lineup_mixture.md`. |
| SIM-04 | 🔬 | Full play-by-play simulator | Play call, outcome, clock, penalty, turnover, fourth-down policy |
| SIM-05 | 🔬 | Counterfactual simulator | Compare coaching decisions, injuries, weather, and matchup changes |
| SIM-06 | 🌙 | Differentiable football environment | Learn policy/state transitions jointly without sacrificing auditability |
| SIM-07 | 🌙 | Multi-agent tactical model | Personnel and scheme interaction below the play level |

Simulation is accepted only if it improves held-out distribution calibration
over simpler margin models. More realism is not automatically more accuracy.

## Phase 8 — paper portfolios and decision science

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| BET-01 | ✅ | Fractional Kelly | Correct American-odds formula and bankroll compounding |
| BET-02 | ✅ | Exposure constraints | Per-bet and per-week caps; simultaneous weekly sizing |
| BET-03 | ✅ | Drawdown analytics | Peak, trough, maximum drawdown, turnover, risk of ruin proxy |
| BET-04 | ✅ | Probability uncertainty haircut | **Implemented 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/probability_uncertainty.py`, `docs/probability_uncertainty_haircut.md`): candidate-specific, side-locked uncertainty accepts either a supplied selected-side probability lower bound or posterior SD plus caller-declared z; sequential, Monte Carlo, and correlated paper sizing retain raw/conservative probability, source, uncertainty, and effective-haircut audit columns. It never changes the forced-pick side, and cannot be ambiguously stacked with the legacy scalar haircut. |
| BET-05 | ✅ | Correlated portfolio sizing | **Implemented 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/portfolio.py`, `docs/correlated_portfolio.md`): deterministic paper-only quadratic fractional-Kelly sizing with signed shared-team factors, opt-in total/weather/market exposures, validated labeled covariance input, and per-position/aggregate/factor caps. |
| BET-06 | ✅ | Monte Carlo bankroll paths | Distribution of terminal wealth/drawdown, not one realized path |
| BET-07 | ✅ | Policy comparison | **Implemented 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/policy_comparison.py`, `docs/policy_comparison.md`): paper-only chronological replay compares flat-unit, configurable confidence-tier, independent quarter-Kelly, and BET-05 risk-constrained quarter-Kelly on identical decisions/outcomes; prediction-level ledgers retain passes, pushes, unresolved games, and zero stakes, with return, bankroll, turnover, and drawdown diagnostics. It ranks and promotes nothing. |
| BET-08 | ✅ | Transaction realism | **Implemented 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/transaction_realism.py`, `docs/transaction_realism.md`): paper execution records apply quote availability/age, kickoff closure, known stake limits, line and price changes, and named fail-closed rejection reasons; settlement uses the executed line/price and preserves pushes and unsettled games. No sportsbook connection or order path exists. |
| BET-09 | ✅ | Responsible-use controls | Paper mode default; prominent limitations and no auto-wager path **Done 2026-09-01 (WP12):** guarded in code -- `tests/test_no_wager_path.py` asserts no `pyproject.toml` dependency matches a sportsbook/exchange wagering-client denylist, no wager-placement verb exists in `src/`/`scripts/` outside the three read-only quote modules (`nfl_ats.odds`, `odds_backfill`, `market_data`), and `docs/responsible_use.md` states the paper-only architecture. Measured: zero wager-placement code and zero wagering-client dependencies today; the guard keeps it that way. (WP12, 2026-09-01) |

## Phase 9 — football pools

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| POL-01 | ✅ | Pool rule configuration | **Completed 2026-09-02** (`src/nfl_ats/pool_workbench.py`, `docs/pool_rules.md`): orthogonal, fail-closed configuration now covers ATS/straight-up picks; standard/confidence/survivor formats; matching scoring methods and explicit correct/incorrect/push points; entry count; confidence assignment; and survivor team reuse/lives. The real forced-pick ATS/opener/one-entry defaults and per-game deadline semantics remain unchanged; factories and mapping inference cover the variants, and rule tiles expose entry count. |
| POL-02 | ✅ | ATS weekly card | Force a pick for every game and rank confidence |
| POL-03 | ✅ | Straight-up probability model | Separate calibrated target, never reuse cover probabilities |
| POL-04 | ❌ | Pick-popularity input | **Closed for lack of data, not effort** (`docs/pool_format_levers.md`). Splash does show a pick distribution, but it unlocks game-by-game as each game kicks off — a deliberate integrity measure, so it is structurally unavailable before ~~the Tuesday lock~~ **each game's real deadline (owner-corrected 2026-08-20: there is no Tuesday pick lock, only a Tuesday LINE lock; refined 2026-08-20: the real per-game deadline is min(kickoff, Sunday 16:00 ET), SNF/MNF locking early — this row's premise should be re-examined, since our own pick for a later game in the week could in principle be informed by an earlier game's already-unlocked pick distribution; not re-assessed here, flagged only)**, and there is no API. The Odds API sells no betting percentages; no free ticket/handle feed offers an API plus history. The only obtainable proxy is the favourite flag, already on the card — and our picks run 54.8% favourites / 46.7% home, so we sit near-neutral against a favourite-loving field anyway |
| POL-05 | 🚧 | Contest utility optimizer | Simulator built and validated against five closed forms (`nfl_ats.pool`, `tests/test_pool.py`, `scripts/pool_levers.py`); independence measured rather than assumed (weekly variance of the correct share is 1.036× binomial over 107 weeks). Results: the format already multiplies a fair share of first place by 2.3×–12.7× at 52.5%; **deliberate contrarianism loses monotonically at every field size** when it costs the full edge (break-even ~2.5 accuracy points per flip), and under a top-15% prize — Splash's usual structure — the lever is neutral at best. A Best Pick ranker is worth +2.4 pp of P(first) at 100 rivals if the recorded +8.7 were real, and **+0.07 pp at the honest +0.9**. **Two accuracy points are worth +11.8 pp.** The format is a multiplier to protect, not a lever to pull. Open only on two observables — the pool's real field size and prize structure — both recordable in Week 1. **2026-09-03:** still queued for the 9/08 lock week; capture alongside the lock, no earlier. **2026-09-11 re-run on the served numbers (`docs/contest_utility_optimizer.md`, `scripts/contest_utility_optimizer.py`, artifact `artifacts/contest_utility_optimizer/20260911T034605Z/`):** the 2026-08-17 read above was scored against a retired card, an arbitrary Best Pick and a hypothetical field, so it was re-run on the measured inputs — ordinary hit rate **52.96%** (1,503 opener-graded games, 107 weeks, `artifacts/opener_evaluation/20260910T211255Z`), served Sunday Best Pick **56.73%** (104 weeks, LEAD-53), our picks on the favourite 46.7%, a fitted hit-rate curve `logit(p) = 0.2392 - 0.02349|spread|`, and the real 285-pick season shape (272 REG in 18 weeks + 13 playoff). Field size and prize structure remain **uncaptured** (`data/pool_observables/` does not exist), so both are declared assumption grids: entries {26, 101, 285, 1001}, payouts {winner-take-all, top 10/15/25%}, field lean {0.50, 0.55, 0.65, 0.75, 0.85}, Best Pick bonus {+1, +2}. The script's detailed simulator ties out **identical to 1e-12** against `simulate_pool_finish` on a shared seed; positive control (perfect card) P(first) 1.000. **Answer: one deviation raises expected payout and it is small and conditional.** (1) Best Pick weighting: at double points the 18 starred picks carry **21.0% of season score variance** and 12.6% of expected score; the star designation itself adds **15.7%** (exact) / **15.9%** [15.1%, 16.9%] (simulated) — at triple points 37.4% / 33.3% / 33.5% [32.2%, 34.8%]. Five-sixths of the variance is ordinary picks. (2) Star position, 285 entries, lean 0.65, all 285 sides held fixed: the ONLY winner is the **contrarian star** (best pick among the games where we hold the underdog) at **+0.20 pp of P(first), 95% [-0.29, +0.60], `probability_positive` 0.85**, costing 0.07 expected points a season = **+0.035 accuracy-point equivalents**; it is monotone in how herded the field is (P+ 0.40 / 0.60 / 0.85 / 0.90 / 0.95 at lean 0.50 / 0.55 / 0.65 / 0.75 / 0.85) and **reverses as the payout widens** (P(paid) +0.17 pp P+ 0.75 winner-take-all, -0.21 P+ 0.375 top-10%, -0.32 P+ 0.20 top-15%, -0.55 P+ 0.05 top-25%). Every other star deviation loses: big-spread -1.69 pp (P+ 0.05, -0.29 acc-pt eq), max-variance -1.39 pp (0.00, -0.24), contrarian-big -1.29 pp (0.15, -0.22), and an **arbitrary** star -0.66 pp (0.00), so having any chooser is worth **+0.11 acc-pt eq**. Side deviation loses too: 10 flips +0.17 pp P+ 0.65 winner-take-all but -0.57 pp top-15% (0.275); 25 flips -0.10 / -2.31 (0.55 / 0.05); 50 flips -1.25 / -6.56 (0.30 / 0.00). (3) Tiebreaker shade: a tiebreak decides whether we are paid in **1.63%** of seasons winner-take-all and **3.87%** at top-15%; times LEAD-54's measured -0.137 pp [-0.935, +0.661] win-rate change that is **-0.0000051 of P(paid), P+ 0.367 = -0.0004 accuracy-point equivalents** — immaterial to three significant figures at every field size, so the shade stays served on LEAD-54's own metric and contest utility has no opinion. (4) **Payout units, the deliverable:** one point of ordinary forced-pick accuracy is worth **+5.80 pp of P(first)** at 285 entries, 95% [+4.67, +6.37], `probability_positive` **1.000**, +11.85 pp of P(top 15%), and **+6.3 finishing percentiles at EVERY field size** (6.13 / 6.30 / 6.32 / 6.34 at 26 / 101 / 285 / 1001) — the field-size-free exchange rate for pricing a research lane. P(first) at 285 entries runs 1.00% / 4.49% / **8.11%** / 13.91% / 21.67% / 42.84% at 50 / 52 / 53 / 54 / 55 / 57% accuracy. (5, added) **LEAD-53's served Sunday Best Pick re-nomination priced in payout units: +2.08 pp of P(first), 95% [+0.83, +3.31], `probability_positive` 1.000 = +0.36 accuracy-point equivalents** — ten times the star-position lever, and the largest contest-format lever in the project; the retired Tuesday nominator was worth +0.11 pp (P+ 0.60, +0.019 acc-pt eq), reproducing LEAD-53's own finding by a second route. Supporting measurement, the only non-simulated cell: accuracy on spreads ≤ 4.5 (53.92%, 829 games) minus spreads ≥ 7.5 (48.31%, 325) = **+5.61 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [-0.80, +12.14], P+ 0.9566**, positive in both season halves (odd +8.59, even +2.56). **Instrument warning, measured:** the field model hands a ZERO-edge card 1.54x a fair share of first when its side profile matches the field's and 2.73x at our actual 46.7% favourite share, so absolute P(first) levels are optimistic and only the paired deltas are quoted. **Decision: change nothing about how the card is submitted today.** Sides do not move; the star rule stays max-probability and switches to the contrarian filter **if and only if** the pool proves winner-take-all (the researched Splash default is ~top 15%, where the arm is measured negative), so the switch waits on an observation, not on more data; the tiebreaker shade is untouched. 13 cells recorded `unresolved_below_power` via `nfl-ats weak-signals record`, family `contest_utility`, category `modeling` (registry 6,002 → 6,015) — twelve are simulated payout quantities converted to ordinary-accuracy-point equivalents and are flagged in every record as **not commensurable** with measured game-level `accuracy_points`; nothing closes, because a Monte Carlo interval conditional on an assumed field cannot refute a mechanism. No rotation window spent, no served behaviour changed. **Still open on exactly the same two observables** (`nfl-ats pool-observables`, LEAD-52): the prize structure alone flips the sign of the one positive deviation found. **2026-09-11 hygiene fix:** a new `payout_first_pp` effect unit was added and the twelve simulated payout cells were re-recorded under it via `--replace` (exact figures from `artifacts/contest_utility_optimizer/20260911T034605Z/`, not the accuracy-point conversion), so `nfl-ats weak-signals pool --league nfl --effect-units accuracy_points` no longer includes them (3,063 → 3,051 eligible) and only `contest_utility_spread_accuracy_gradient` remains from this family in that pool. |
