| POL-09 | 🚧 | Best Pick ranker | The pool pays one Best Pick per week and our confidence ordering is flat (top-\|residual\| scored 48.6% over 107 weeks). Three signals were predeclared and screened once on the registry window [2013, 2015] (August 2026, `docs/best_pick_ranker.md`): `calibrated_probability` (−8.16 points vs all-pick, `probability_positive` 0.0925) and `key_number_distance` (−6.19, 0.170) both made the weekly top-1 pick **worse** and are closed; `sweep_robustness` scored +5.57 points at 0.7955, clearing the predeclared 0.75 screen gate. Its earned opener confirmation then ran once on [2020, 2021] with the deployed `player` profile: **top-1 60.0% (21/35) vs 51.32% all-pick, +8.68 points, week-blocked [−7.00, +22.88], `probability_positive` 0.865** — clears the predeclared 0.75 gate; ~~verdict `confirmed`~~ **verdict `unresolved`** (downgraded 2026-08-18, see the re-read below), both windows now spent. Consequence per the predeclaration: **use `sweep_robustness` to choose the Best Pick in 2026** — a pool-play decision, no activation and no model change. Two disjoint windows, two grades, same direction; but 86 top-1 picks total and a rank correlation of +0.067 (p=0.099), so expect regression toward the all-pick rate. **Re-read 2026-08-17 (`docs/pool_format_levers.md`): every recorded figure reproduces exactly, but the confirmation is mostly the TIE-BREAK, not the signal.** `sweep_robustness` is a half-point width censored at 8.0, so weeks routinely tie at the top and `select_best_pick` breaks ties alphabetically by `game_id`. **24 of the 35 confirmation weeks were ties** (39 of 51 on the screen). Scoring the same signal under a random tie-break gives **52.24%, delta +0.92 points — not 60.0% / +8.68**; the recorded value sits at the 96th percentile of the tie-break distribution. On the screen window the tie-break pushed the other way (+9.05 rather than the recorded +5.57), so *direction* survives but the "two windows, same direction, both clearing" argument is partly alphabetical coincidence. Also unmeasurable by construction: top-1 standard error is **8.45 points on 35 weeks**, so anything in [43%, 62%] is indistinguishable from an arbitrary nomination, and resolving a 5-point effect needs ~384 weeks ≈ 21 seasons. **This re-read re-scores nothing, but it DOES change the registry verdict**: `best_pick_ranker_opener`'s `[2020, 2021]` window is downgraded `confirmed` → `unresolved` (2026-08-18; tie-agnostic delta +0.92 points, honest `probability_positive` **0.536-0.554** under D2 widening — never near the predeclared 0.75 gate). Keep using the signal to choose the weekly Best Pick regardless — it is still the right card to play, since both alternatives (`calibrated_probability`, `key_number_distance`) are measured negatives and the pool is forced picks — but budget the edge at +0.9 points, not +8.68. The published card and dashboard now disclose a tie whenever one occurs. **Promotion moved the tie onto the card that is actually played.** 2026 Week 1 on the active `weak_stack` card IS tied: `2026_01_ARI_LAC` and `2026_01_WAS_PHI` both score 8.0, next 5.5, so the published nomination of ARI is the alphabetical `game_id` tie-break rather than a lean (verified 2026-08-18 on `artifacts/margin_predictions/2026-week-01-20260818T013139Z`, filtered to `market_residual`). The retired `player` card was the untied one, at 7.0 with 6.5 next; the earlier note here had the two cards the wrong way round. ~~**The disclosure claim above is true only of the dashboard** — `dashboard/app_pages/picks.py` computes the tie note, `publishing.py` has no tie logic, so the tracked `CURRENT_PREDICTIONS.md` presents an arbitrary nomination as if it were a lean. Being fixed~~ **Fix shipped and tested 2026-08-18** (`docs/week1_readiness.md` §2): the shared computation was extracted into `nfl_ats.best_pick.best_pick_tie_count`/`best_pick_tie_note`, `publishing.py` now threads the tie note through `_forecast_best_pick` → `_publication_context` → `_publication_header`/`_best_pick_note`, and `publish_active_predictions`'s return payload carries `best_pick_tied: bool`. Proven by `test_published_card_discloses_a_tied_best_pick` and `test_published_card_does_not_disclose_an_unambiguous_best_pick` (`tests/test_publishing.py`), and verified against the real, live, tied Week 1 card via a redirected `--destination`/`--readme` run that touched no tracked file. **The tracked card itself, `CURRENT_PREDICTIONS.md`, is deliberately left stale** — republishing it competes with the ledger-contamination decision, so regeneration is deferred pending the `docs/week1_readiness.md` §owner-decision item on the 16 contaminated CLV ledger rows, not done as part of this fix. **2026-08-18 (owner decision): the weekly NOMINATION rule switches to a measured winner** — a same-day screen (`scratchpad/bestpick_opener/`, `scripts/best_pick_opener_ranker_eval.py`) found the alpha=2000 candidate probability's distance from 0.5, restricted to that week's below-median cross-book opener dispersion (fallback to the full week on missing/degenerate data), +3.92 points vs its unfiltered parent, `probability_positive` 0.813, interval [−3.92, +11.76], 102 paired weeks — third reuse of the same 107-week population, still unresolved, but the strongest lean measured in either screen and EV-positive against the now-signal-free `sweep_robustness` incumbent on a forced pick. Sides never change, only which game is nominated. New module `src/nfl_ats/best_pick_nomination.py` (`best_pick.py` stays frozen); the production tie-break composes chooser 6's filter with a SEPARATE chooser's dispersion tie-break, a combination never itself scored (flagged in both the module and `docs/best_pick_ranker.md`). 2026 scores both arms: v1 via the existing `is_best_pick` ledger flag (unchanged), v2 via a new `best_pick_nomination_v2` prospective challenger (`artifacts/prospective/challengers.json`). See `docs/best_pick_ranker.md` § "2026-08-18: the weekly NOMINATION rule switches" **Composed rule scored as played, 2026-09-07 evening (lane U, Fable; coordinator measured the 6 registry rows under `pol09_best_pick_composed_v1` and re-ran its tests; docs/best_pick_composed_rule.md, scripts/best_pick_composed_rule_eval.py):** the never-scored composition (chooser-6 filter + dispersion tie-break) on the same 107-week opener archive (fourth reuse, no window), scored on the ACTIVE model's pick side (the 2026-08-19 v3 audit had scored it on the alpha=2000 candidate arm's side, which differs in 255 of 1,537 games; the candidate-arm row reproduces that audit's 54.37% as a tie-out): **v2 as played 56.31% top-1 (58/103)** vs v1 sweep 55.66% (+0.97 pts [-10.68, +11.65], P+ 0.536; nominee differs in 69 weeks, outcome in 35) and vs raw top-|residual| 52.83% (+3.88 [-6.80, +14.56], P+ 0.729); with the 10+ spread discount 57.28% (+0.97 vs v2 as played [-2.91, +4.85], P+ 0.592, on a 3-2 split of the 5 weeks it changes). The tie-break layer is inert: v2 differs from chooser 6 alone in 2 of 107 weeks; the pool ties in 5 weeks, dispersion differs from alphabetical in exactly 1 (lost). All six cells `unresolved_below_power`. Week 1 reproduces MIA +3.5 through the production module, untied. Decision: the served rule stays; the discount stays the side-ledger challenger it already is (P+ 0.592 on five weeks is not re-plumbed the night before lock). **2026-09-11:** superseded in practice by LEAD-53 (Sunday-morning Best Pick re-nomination, served since 2026-09-10, ranks by the played side's refreshed cover probability at the frozen line) and POL-05's fitted hit-rate curve; this row stays open only for the opener-time ranker comparison (`artifacts/best_pick_opener_ranker`), which has no served consumer. |
| POL-10 | 🚧 | Prospective 2026 evidence | Grade the active model and every frozen challenger on games nobody has looked at, at BOTH grades (the recorded/opener line primary, close secondary). Built 2026-08-17, three weeks before Week 1 locks (`docs/prospective_evidence.md`), because prospective scoring is the only way to settle `mod07_weak_signal_stack` (unresolved, P+ 0.8745) and firm up `best_pick_ranker` (60.0% on 35 top-1 picks) **without spending either of the two remaining opener windows** -- and it only produces evidence if picks are recorded before kickoff every week from Week 1, so a season that goes unrecorded is simply gone. Three gaps closed: nothing computed whether a 2026 pick WON (the MKT-04 ledger scored line movement only, never joining `result`); the weekly Best Pick was recomputed at render time and overwritten every publish, so Week 1's nomination would have ceased to exist once Week 2 published; and the MOD-07 stack was not registered, so no challenger card was being produced at all. Now: `nfl-ats prospective-score` settles at both grades reusing `clv.pick_correct` so FND-04 push semantics are literally the same function; `is_best_pick` persists in `PAPER_DECISION_COLUMNS`, written only while EVERY game of the week is still ahead, first-write-wins, exactly one per week enforced on read; challengers resolve by configuration fingerprint rather than directory recency (the baseline and challenger share an artifacts directory, so newest-wins would have silently recorded the baseline's picks as the challenger's); and `weekly-run` gained four optional trailing steps whose failure never aborts the fail-closed publish. **Anti-backdating is enforced twice** -- refused at write, and re-asserted at scoring, so a hand-written row cannot be laundered into evidence by running the scorer. A Week 1 challenger card was generated as a rehearsal and the two arms disagreed on 3 of 16 games. **The rehearsal rows were then reset (2026-08-17)** so that Week 1's first ledger write is the real Tuesday-lock card on 2026-09-08 — the MKT-04 ledger anchors each game at its FIRST publication, which would otherwise have scored August's picks at August's lines instead of what was actually entered. **2026-08-19: the challenger ledger grew to 8 ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE entries** — `mod07_weak_signal_stack`, `hc_year_one_fade_overlay`, `best_pick_nomination_v2`, plus five new frozen pick-level overlays built this session at zero window cost: `injury_value_lost_tilt_overlay` (parameter-free sign tilt on the registry's strongest lean, +1.32 pts P+ 0.8875), `division_revenge_tilt_overlay` (both grades lean the revenge side, P+ 0.88/0.86), `backup_qb_fade_overlay` (backup side under-covers at both grades, P+ 0.17/0.10), `surface_switch_tilt_overlay` (cross-league-replicated surface lead, see ENV-02), and `spread_gap_zone_fade_overlay` (model picks at |line| 7.5-10 hit 46-48% on three separate windows incl. a never-mined 2011-2017 replication at P+ 0.914; bounds frozen before the replication). Each is tracked independently against the un-flipped active card in `_cmd_publish_predictions` (8 additive fail-open blocks); none touches the production pick path; all eight ride the same **`--record-decisions` requirement on the Sep 8 lock-day run**. **2026-08-20: challenger ledger grew from 11 active to 16 ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE (19 entries total; measured, `artifacts/prospective/challengers.json`).** Four new this session, all dual-tracked, no-window-cost: `interim_hc_first_game_tilt_overlay` (see PER-07), `forecast_weather_kn_warm_team_cold_late_tilt` and `forecast_weather_kn_precip_high_total_tilt` (see ENV-01), `injury_signal_refresh_tilt` (below). The Sep 8 lock-day run must adjudicate all 16, not the 8 the previous session's note describes. **Two research docs sharpened WHERE the already-wired observed-movement policy's prospective edge concentrates** (`docs/movement_attribution.md`, `docs/opener_error_analysis.md`), building on `docs/observed_movement_channel.md`'s production `refresh-picks`/Sunday-realism rule (committed `9d70817`, itself not yet described in this file's phase tables -- flagged, not backfilled here). Attribution: of 494 games where Tuesday-to-close movement disagreed with the model's opener pick, injury news explains the largest share -- following the market on the 123 games with a pregame skill-position injury asymmetry AND `\|open_move\| >= 1.0` is worth **+17.07 accuracy points, week-blocked 95% [+0.79, +31.67], P+ 0.976** (a correlated decomposition of the already-recorded `observed_movement_threshold_1_0` entry, not independently poolable with it). Weather-attributed moves lean the OPPOSITE way (P+ 0.11-0.19, thin n=24-39). About 42-48% of the disagreement GAMES show no visible cause in these three archives, but those games carry a smaller share of the total flip VALUE, not half of it -- value-weighted, the unattributed share is ~19% unfiltered and ~36% at the threshold cut, where it still scores positive rather than zero (+8.20 pts, P+ 0.797, n=122) -- the market knows things outside these three archives too, just less of it than a naive game-count split would suggest. `injury_signal_refresh_tilt` operationalizes the injury-attribution finding as a live challenger reading Wed/Thu/Fri practice-status filings. Separately, a 12-family mined battery of the active model's own opener-grade errors (`docs/opener_error_analysis.md`, 24 cells recorded) found and CORRECTED a label-swap bug: an apparent contrarian "fade the market when it confirms our pick" lead was backwards -- read correctly, the model does BETTER when observed movement agrees with its pick (+3.60 pts) and WORSE when it disagrees (-5.99 pts, whole week-blocked CI below zero), which is the *same* mechanism the production movement policy already exploits, not a new contrarian overlay; the corrected finding sharpens where that policy's edge concentrates rather than adding a new one. The battery's strongest surviving lead is a big-spread dampener: the model loses accuracy at opener spreads of 10+ points (-7.91 pts, whole week-blocked CI below zero, P+ 0.020) and 7-9.5 (-4.38 pts), while the two smallest spread buckets are both positive -- a candidate Best-Pick-eligibility discount. **2026-09-03 correction:** the "not yet built" above is stale — the discount was built 2026-08-20 as the `best_pick_big_spread_eligibility` side-ledger challenger (`docs/best_pick_big_spread_challenger.md`, `ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE`, 10+ spreads excluded from v2's pool with fallback; Week 1 stays MIA +3.5 under both rules). **2026-09-03 readiness:** lockday rehearsal PASS (29 active, 0 errors), scheduler daemon alive, refresh path fails closed pre-lock. First genuine rows land at the 9/08 lock. **2026-09-11: the first prospective grades exist and are on the site.** Measured this session with `nfl-ats prospective-score` after fixing its outcome source (it had been reading only the processed feature table, which has no 2026 `result`, so every entrant reported 0 settled; it now overlays `artifacts/settlement/game_results.parquet`): on the two finished Week 1 games (SEA 13 NE 10, SF 27 LA 7) the active model is 2-0 at the decision line, the base model without pick overlays 2-0, and among 39 challenger arms 0 are ahead of the card, 23 match it pick for pick and 8 are behind -- worst `movement_rule_composed_v1` at 0-2, then seven at 1-1 or 0-1-1. 14 of 16 recorded picks are still pending, so nothing here resolves anything; it is the first measurement, not a verdict. Every arm's 2026 record now shows under its name in the Model page ledger, and the History page carries the same numbers in "This season so far". **2026-09-11 first settled games (measured):** `settle_thu` re-run by hand — 965 graded rows across 72 arms, 104 settled, 861 pending. Both played games graded at every ledger: NE at SEA (SEA by 3 at NE +3.5, card won) and SF at LA (SF by 20 at SF +3.5, card won); every ACTIVE_PROSPECTIVE challenger has a graded row for both. One grading caveat to carry: five overlay arms (`hc_year_one_fade_overlay`, `injury_value_lost_tilt_overlay`, `division_revenge_tilt_overlay`, `surface_switch_tilt_overlay`, `spread_gap_zone_fade_overlay`) were recorded 2026-09-08 16:35 UTC at NE +3.0, before the pool posted +3.5, so their NE at SEA rows grade as a push where the pool's line is a win; the rows are post-kickoff and `--replace-week` leaves them, so their Week 1 tallies carry a one-game line mismatch (owner decision from 2026-09-09 still pending). **2026-09-11 (LEAD-29 lane):** challenger set grew with `interim_playcaller_first_game_back_overlay` — 14 Week 1 rows, all mirrors of the served pick. |
| POL-11 | 🚧 | Observed-movement late-week pick refresh | **Built and wired live 2026-08-20** (`docs/late_week_refresh.md`, `src/nfl_ats/pick_refresh.py`, CLI `nfl-ats refresh-picks`) -- a production feature with no prior row in this file, flagged rather than silently added by two independent passes this session. Owner correction 2026-08-20: pool picks are editable up to each game's own kickoff; only the grading LINE freezes at the Tuesday-noon lock -- a previously-unspent structural edge, since a Friday injury designation, a kickoff-nearest weather forecast, or simply a fresher model run can inform a pick the frozen Tuesday line never had a chance to price. Four named refresh passes (Thursday pre-TNF, Saturday, Sunday morning before 4:00pm ET) recompute the active model's probability with current features but always GRADE against the original Tuesday card (read from the paper-decision ledger, never rewritten in place); the real per-game deadline is `min(kickoff, that week's Sunday 16:00 ET)`, so SNF/MNF picks lock early with the rest of the week. One market-based decision rule IS applied to the actual played pick, distinct from every other pick-level overlay (those stay challenger-tracked only, never touching the real pick): if the currently-captured line has moved `>=1.0` point from the frozen Tuesday line, the refreshed pick follows the market's side; otherwise the model's own recomputed pick stands. **The evidence base** (`docs/observed_movement_channel.md`, 6 predeclared cells, week-blocked bootstrap seed 20260819, paired against the production pick on the same 1,503-game opener archive): `observed_movement_threshold_1_0` (Tuesday-to-close, full slate) +1.863 pts, 95% [-0.469, +4.267], P+ 0.935; `observed_movement_oracle_full_slate` +1.730 pts, [-1.110, +4.564], P+ 0.883; the deadline-respecting Sunday-morning-realism variant (2023-2025, n=799 -- a measured LOWER bound, since the `intraday_hourly` archive's real coverage ceiling is ~10:55 ET Sunday, well before the true 16:00 ET deadline) reads stronger: `observed_movement_threshold_1_0_sunday_am_realism` **+3.254 pts, 95% [+0.251, +6.267], P+ 0.981**. All six entries `unresolved_below_power` -- no positive control has been run for this channel (so none is `bounded_by_control`), and no whole interval sits below zero (so none is `wrong_sign_resolved`). Per AGENTS.md's "a promotion bar is not a decision bar," this is an EV decision, not a claim the channel is resolved: `probability_positive` 0.935-0.981 clears the standing forced-pick EV bar, so the policy is wired and executing, not merely proposed. The 1.0-point threshold is the stronger of the predeclared {0.5, 1.0} grid at both readings, fixed before either number was seen, not tuned after the fact. **2026-08-20 same-day follow-up sharpens WHERE this edge concentrates; it does not re-decide whether to play it** (full figures under POL-10): `docs/movement_attribution.md` attributes most of the Tuesday-to-close disagreement games to injury news -- the ≥1.0-threshold, injury-flagged subset reads +17.07 pts, P+ 0.976, a correlated decomposition of `observed_movement_threshold_1_0`, not independently poolable with it -- and wires a dedicated `injury_signal_refresh_tilt` challenger that front-runs the Wed/Thu/Fri practice-report signal instead of waiting for the close itself to move. `docs/opener_error_analysis.md` independently confirms the same mechanism after fixing a label-swap bug: the model covers 56.96% when observed movement already agrees with its pick vs. 47.37% when it disagrees (whole week-blocked CI below zero on the disagreement side, but this describes where the active model underperforms, not a beat-the-baseline candidate, so still `unresolved_below_power` by design). Deliberately deferred, not built: wiring any research overlay (injury value-lost, division revenge, etc.) into the refresh's own decision path, and settling `final_pick_per_game` against real outcomes inside `prospective-score` (needs a real week of refresh data first) **Inactives (T-90) channel, 2026-09-01 (WP5 design + WP17 build):** feasibility study `docs/inactives_channel.md` -- 238/272 (87.5%) of 2026 REG games are playable under `pick_refresh.pick_deadline` (SNF/MNF structurally excluded; the Sunday 16:05-17:00 ET slot IS playable, computed not assumed); `nfl.com/inactives/` chosen as primary source (robots clear; mirrors the `/injuries/` scraper), RotoWire fallback; ESPN's JSON API and nflverse confirmed NOT to carry a T-90 inactives feed; `snap_counts.parquet` zero-snap rows are a leak-free grading label for a backtest but never a pregame feature. Built: `src/nfl_ats/inactives_capture.py` + `scripts/capture_inactives.py` (point-in-time snapshots under `data/players/inactives/<stamp>/`), seven `inactives_*` scheduler rows at each slot's T-90 window, 18 tests + 7 scheduler pins. Caveat stated plainly: nfl.com served only its preseason placeholder all session and Wayback was unreachable, so the populated-page parser is inferred by analogy to `/injuries/` and exits non-zero with `empty_reason="unrecognized_page_structure"` the first time a real page does not match. Not done: wiring the capture into `refresh-picks` (the existing `refresh_sun` 10:00 ET pass fires before both Sunday inactives windows) and the predeclared Section 5 experiment; no window spent. (WP17, 2026-09-01) **2026-09-03 refresh-path verification:** `refresh-picks --season 2026 --week 1` (no `--record-decisions`) fails closed with "No recorded original card" — the Tuesday ledger does not exist until the 9/08 lock, so there is nothing to refresh against and nothing was written. **2026-09-07 (measured):** the "not done" wiring above is stale -- `refresh-picks` runs `record_inactives_refresh_overlay` on every pass (src/nfl_ats/cli_commands/publishing.py), and the seven `refresh_*_inactives_*` scheduler jobs (added 2026-09-02) fire 5 minutes after each inactives capture closes; all seven were exercised by hand today via `capture_scheduler.py --run-job` and stop at the correct "no Tuesday ledger yet" guard. Also fixed today: `refresh-picks` no longer requires `--season/--week` (defaults to the active forecast), which is what had made every scheduled pass die on 2026-09-06. **2026-09-11 Friday audit (measured by the coordinator):** the served surfaces disagreed on which side is being played. The pick-revision ledger's last state (Thursday passes, `artifacts/prospective/pick_revisions.parquet`) had DAL at NYG -> NYG (coach fade after the model's own read crossed by 0.07), ATL at PIT -> PIT and WAS at PHI -> PHI (leading books moved a full point, follow rule), and a read-only `refresh-picks` pass this morning reproduces all three; but the 2026-09-11 01:18 ET `publish-predictions` republish (served the pass-protection crosswalk fix: NO at DET -> NO, DEN at KC -> DEN) rewrote the card table from a fresh compute that does not run the refresh-time follow rule and dropped the late-week section, so the tracked card and the site's picks table showed DAL and WAS while the site's what-changed table said NYG and PHI 'are the ones being played'. Fixed in code, not by hand: (1) the board's picks table now renders the latest recorded revision's side per game (decision score recomputed for that side, floored at 50%), so the 'has not caught up' footnote no longer fires; (2) `refresh-picks --publish-card` lists every eligible game whose current side differs from the published table (`RefreshedGame.published_pick_side`, `card_changed_games`), not only this pass's delta, and says the section wins where it disagrees with the table — run for real (`--note friday_audit_republish`), 3 rows written to `CURRENT_PREDICTIONS.md`. **Still open, blocked on a permission the session could not obtain:** the paper-decision ledger still holds only the lock state (16 rows, recorded 2026-09-09 20:28 UTC), so `refresh-picks` freezes the lock's composition flags and treats NO at DET / DEN at KC as DET / KC while the card serves NO / DEN; the designed repair is `nfl-ats publish-predictions --record-decisions --replace-week --no-board` (drops pre-kickoff rows in every ledger with a timestamped .bak, leaves the two played games untouched, re-records the served state). Rule to keep: a mid-week `publish-predictions` that changes a served rule must carry `--record-decisions --replace-week` in the same command, otherwise the ledger, the refresh baseline and the card diverge. **Same day, later (measured):** the Friday lineups job's daily `publish-predictions --with-board` rewrote the card at 16:14 UTC and dropped the late-week section again, so `scripts/refresh_lineup_forecast.py` now runs `refresh-picks --publish-card --note lineups_refresh` and `publish-board` after every daily republish; the section was re-appended by hand for today (3 rows). **Same day, consistency guard (lane; live output reported by the lane):** `nfl-ats card-ledger-check` (`src/nfl_ats/card_ledger_check.py`) compares the paper ledger's side and composition flag per pre-kickoff game against a fresh `current_played_card_view` (extracted from `record_paper_decisions`, same derivation) and the pick-revision ledger's last state against the board's rendered side; exit 1 on any disagreement. Live 2026-09-11: 7 disagreements — NO at DET and DEN at KC on side and flag, ATL at PIT and DAL at NYG on the composition flag alone (inputs moved further since the morning entry); zero ledger-vs-board disagreements. Wired as a never-failing warning into `refresh-picks` JSON, logged after the daily lineup refit's publish, and printed by `publish-predictions` with the repair command whenever it serves without `--record-decisions`. The repair itself (`--record-decisions --replace-week --no-board`) is still unrun. |
| POL-12 | 🚧 | Tiebreaker score guess | The pool breaks ties on the final score of the week's LAST game (owner, 2026-09-01; Week 1: DEN @ KC, Monday). Shipped same day: `nfl-ats tiebreaker` (`src/nfl_ats/tiebreaker.py`, `tests/test_tiebreaker.py`) — market-implied score from the freshest local odds snapshot (median across books, totals captured every run since `odds-ingest --markets spreads,h2h,totals`), calibrated against all 4,630 lined finals 2009–2025 via a widening (margin, total) neighborhood; reports the closest-total-optimal median guess AND the neighborhood's most common exact finals, because the pool's tiebreak metric is not recorded anywhere — capture it in Week 1 alongside POL-05's field-size/prize observables. Honest error bars printed with every guess (measured 2026-09-01): market total MAE 10.5 (median 9.0, bias +0.5 — actuals run half a point over), implied per-team score MAE ~7.4. **Open: the owner-anticipated over/under training regime — design FROZEN 2026-09-01 in `docs/totals_model.md`** (predeclared before any totals fit: market-residual target, explicit 40-column pregame allowlist from the canonical table, production's exact impute→scale→Ridge(10) pipeline, expanding walk-forward min-500, MAE blend sweep with `clv.week_blocked_bootstrap`, registry recording, `TOTALS_RESIDUAL_WEIGHT` wiring into the tiebreaker). Execution is one session of work; owner confirmed queued-not-now (2026-09-01). The model's margin blend already ships: the guess margin = market + 0.2 × the active model's disagreement, the 0.2 measured on 1,537 opener-graded games (`tiebreaker.MODEL_RESIDUAL_WEIGHT` docstring carries the sweep) — the raw model is WORSE than the market as a point estimate (MAE 10.00 vs 9.91) despite beating it on sides, which calibrates expectations for the totals regime too **Regime RUN (WP1, 2026-09-01).** `docs/totals_model.md` executed as frozen: `src/nfl_ats/totals.py`, `nfl-ats totals-backtest`, `tests/test_totals.py`, one backtest (`artifacts/totals_backtest/20260901T184010Z`), one registry entry, the tiebreaker wiring. Measured on 3,935 walk-forward REG games 2010-2025: the raw model total is WORSE than the market total (MAE 10.5495 vs 10.4249), replicating the margin axis; the MAE-minimising blend `total_line + 0.1 * predicted_residual` is worth +0.0008 total points, week-blocked 95% [-0.0062, +0.0077], `probability_positive` 0.583 over 261 week blocks -- `unresolved_below_power`, recorded `totals_market_residual_blend` (family `totals_market_residual`, units `mae_improvement`). `tiebreaker.TOTALS_RESIDUAL_WEIGHT = 0.1` is derived from that sweep; playoffs reported separately (188 games, +0.0237), never pooled. **Wave 2 (WP18, same day):** wave 1's 41 columns plus 24 drive-pace columns from `game_features_pbp.parquet` (`docs/totals_model_wave2.md`, positive control P+ 1.000): wave 2's blend beats wave 1's on a paired per-game basis by +0.0020 MAE points, week-blocked 95% [-0.0024, +0.0063], **P+ 0.8235** -- the EV favourite; recorded `totals_market_residual_wave2_vs_wave1`. **Wiring applied 2026-09-02:** `src/nfl_ats/tiebreaker.py` prefers `totals_wave2` when `game_features_pbp.parquet` is present, retains the independently selected 0.1 blend weight, and fails closed to market-only rather than silently substituting wave 1 when the present wave-2 input is stale, incomplete, or misaligned; an entirely absent PBP table keeps an explicitly labelled wave-1 fresh-clone fallback. POL-12 remains in progress only because the pool's actual tiebreak metric still needs its Week 1 capture. **Tiebreaker neighborhood fixed (WP14, same day):** the totals blend surfaced a half-point-quantization edge -- a +0.042 nudge to the centre dropped the whole 38-game `total_line == 41.5` bucket and moved the published Week 1 guess DOWN two points while the model argued higher. The calibration neighborhood is now kernel-weighted (`max(0, 1 - d)`, `d = sqrt((dmargin/1.0)^2 + (dtotal/1.5)^2)`, bandwidths inherited from the old first window, widened continuously until the Kish effective sample size reaches the same 150 floor; weighted medians and weighted exact-final counts). Measured: the guess now moves monotonically with the line (41 -> 42 -> 43 as the blended line rises 43.00 -> 43.50, versus the old 43 -> 41 -> 43). Consequence stated plainly: the Week 1 DEN@KC card reads **KC 22 - DEN 19** (was KC 23 - DEN 20) at the unblended market total as well, a 2-point difference far inside the 10.5-point market-total MAE. (WP1, 2026-09-01) **LEAD-54's -1 low-side shade wired into the served guess (2026-09-11).** Read `docs/tiebreaker_low_side_shading.md`'s decision (the predeclared -1 shade at `probability_positive` 0.816 full-sample / 0.843 out-of-sample on the closest-total AE metric, nothing resolved against it): `src/nfl_ats/tiebreaker.py` now adds `TOTAL_LOW_SIDE_SHADE_POINTS = -1.0` (source-tagged `TOTAL_LOW_SIDE_SHADE_SOURCE`) to `build_report`'s `guess_total_line` immediately after the existing market+model blend, so the neighborhood, the pick-consistent score lattice and the fallback median guess all recentre on the same, now one-point-lower, served total -- the margin blend (`MODEL_RESIDUAL_WEIGHT` 0.2) and the totals blend that produces the pre-shade total are untouched, and the score split still preserves the projected margin. The existing pick-side-strict, lattice-consistency guard (`nfl_ats.score_lattice.pick_consistent_top_score`, `TiebreakerConsistencyError`, publishing fails closed) is unchanged, just handed the shaded target. The shade is recorded, not narrated: two new `TiebreakerReport` fields (`low_side_shade_points`, `low_side_shade_source`) surface in `tiebreaker.json` (`total_low_side_shade_points`/`_source`) and as a new `total_low_side_shade` lineage source; reader-facing text carries only the already-shaded score, no jargon added. Measured (read-only rehearsal against the live checkout, `tiebreaker_report()`, nothing written): the week's last game DEN @ KC (2026-09-14, Monday), market total 43.5 -- served total moves **43.72 -> 42.72**; the printed integer guess stays **KC 23 - DEN 20 (total 43)** in both cases this week, because the pick-consistent lattice's nearest admissible cell (margin exactly 3, the only feasible parity near the KC -2.5 centre) sits at total 43 whichever side of it the continuous target lands on -- a disclosed case of the shade moving the underlying number without moving this week's discrete score, not evidence the wiring is inert (the fallback-branch and model-blend unit tests below do show the printed guess moving on finer lattices). Tests edited to match, per the moratorium (no new test files/functions): `tests/test_tiebreaker.py` (six assertions plus one rewritten comparison block), `tests/test_served_total.py` and `tests/test_totals.py` (two assertions each plus an import), `tests/test_played_card_lineage.py` (two exact-list lineage-source assertions widened, and its 3-row `_finals()` fixture broadened to 17 scores after the shade pushed that tiny fixture's lattice construction to exactly zero feasible mass -- a fixture-size artifact, not a production issue). Gates measured this session: `ruff format --check .` / `ruff check .` clean on every file this lane touched (two pre-existing, unrelated failures remain in `scripts/playcaller_change_screen.py` and `scripts/sharp_book_weighted_movement.py`); `mypy src` -- Success: no issues found in 218 source files; `pytest tests -k tiebreak -n 2` -- 75 passed. |

New `src/nfl_ats/play_probability.py`: a walk-forward, isotonic-calibrated `HistGradientBoostingClassifier` for P(plays)/P(starts) using depth-chart rank, this week's own injury report, recent snap history, roster status, and (for QBs) the team's own QB1's injury status. Archived nflverse's full (all-position) depth-chart history for the first time -- `nfl_ats.quarterbacks`'s existing archives are QB-only -- handling a measured schema seam (legacy week-labelled rows through 2024, daily dt-timestamped rows from 2025) and nesting the archive under `data/players/raw/depth_charts/` rather than `data/players/raw/<stamp>/` after a measured collision with `nfl_ats.players.latest_player_snapshot`'s glob. **Measured** (`scripts/build_play_probability_panel.py`, 391,045 depth-chart-row-weeks, 2013-2025, `nfl_ats.play_probability.walk_forward_evaluate`): overall Brier 0.040 (full information) / 0.099 (serving-realistic, `roster_status` forced to `"ACT"` to match what is actually available at serve time) vs. 0.176 (depth-rank-only baseline) vs. 0.221 (the current base-rate approach lane X built). Season-blocked bootstrap (12 seasons), serving-realistic vs. the current approach: +0.124 Brier points, 95% [0.102, 0.146], probability_positive 1.0 -- recorded via `nfl-ats weak-signals record` as `play_probability_model_brier_improvement` (`unresolved_below_power`; positive on 12/12 season blocks but one predeclared measurement, not a positive-control-bounded closure). Calibration by depth slot is tight (within 1 point for every slot but the small-n `WR4+`/`other`). `scripts/build_week_lineups.py` now scores every `gsis_id` player through this model (`probability_source: "play_probability_model"`, new `start_probability` field); the active margin model's own forecast input for its scored QB survives as a separate, never-deleted `model_qb_start_probability` field. The line moved 1 point toward PHI."). A row whose new side has not reached the picks table above is marked "not in the picks table yet", and one line under the table names those games and says the side shown there is the one being played. When Tuesday's card is not on file, the ledger alone can still populate the section, and the nothing-changed state stays a single sentence rather than an empty box. `board_content._build_week_changes` / `_late_week_change_reason` / `_catch_up_note` and `board_terminal._week_changes_section`; no new tests (moratorium), 210 existing board tests green, full suite 4,527 passed with two failures owned by other lanes (scheduler argv timing, registry explorer). `publish-board` re-run: index.html grew 1,057 bytes on the same forecast, three rows to five plus the catch-up line. **2026-09-10 (UI-20 lane, second pass): every row of "What changed this week" now names a football reason instead of a placeholder.** The mid-week DAL at NYG revision had the ledger policy "the model recomputed" and a zero line move, so its published reason was the empty sentence "The computer's own read on this game moved." New `nfl_ats.pick_revision_factors` rebuilds the factor decomposition of the model's market-residual read at the forecast the locked card was built from and at the forecast the refresh produced -- each forecast folder stores its own feature values, so the two reads are decomposed through one refit of that week's ridge (measured 0.15 s to fit, 0.27 s for the whole lookup) -- diffs the per-family point contributions, and names the one or two families that moved most toward the new side using the phrases the site already uses (`market_decomposition.FAMILY_PHRASES`). Measured for DAL at NYG: the model's read moved 0.073 points toward Dallas, all of it from expected player availability (-0.055) and estimated value lost to injuries (-0.018), because New York picked up offense, offensive-line, skill and special-teams unavailability on Thursday's report; that crossed the raw read from the Giants (0.5019) to the Cowboys (0.4996), and because the coach-fade adjustment plays this game against the model's read, the served pick moved to NYG. Rendered: "The computer's own read crossed over to DAL -- a 0.07-point move, mostly on expected player availability and estimated value lost to injuries. The coach fade adjustment plays this game against that read, so the card moved to NYG." Guards, all runtime assertions rather than tests: the refit must reproduce each stored residual through `family_contributions_from_ridge`, the non-model part of the stored residual (the served home-side offset) must be identical in both reads, and the raw read on each side must reconstruct the ledger's previous and new pick sides -- any failure falls back to the old sentence. The two overlay rows (NO at DET, DEN at KC) gained the same treatment from the other end: their trigger was already named ("pass-protection mismatch"), so they now add which side the computer's own read is on -- "The computer's own read still favours DET, so the card is taken the other way." `publish-board` re-run: index.html +353 bytes, exactly three changed spans, no other page touched; no new tests (moratorium), 200 board tests and the full suite 4,529 passed / 9 skipped. **2026-09-10, the "why this pick" panel was three weeks and one model stale.** Measured: `artifacts/waterfall_feed/latest.json` pointed at a feed built 2026-08-22 for model `3083f6cbc5e45acb` on feature table `0a18e2d9...`, while the active model is `d49194e04945a5e5` on `2fb3451b...`; `board_content._build_attribution` rendered it into every game's attribution panel on index.html with no model or digest check, so the per-factor points a reader saw came from a model the card no longer runs. The only thing that regenerated the feed, `scripts/waterfall_feed.py`, had been deleted in the repository cut (b7ed31d). Restored from `b7ed31d^` and stripped of comments and docstrings; it needed three repairs, all of them the card moving on without it. (1) The served big-spread home-side correction (`home_side_offset_big_spreads_v2`) had been added to the published centre after the script was written, so its bit-exact reproduction guard fired at 0.785 points on the two games with spreads in the 7.5-10 bucket (ARI at LAC, CLE at JAX); the builder now reads the card's own `home_side_offset.json` sidecar, reconciles against centre-plus-offset, and carries the correction as its own labelled waterfall step rather than hiding it in the market or calibration line. (2) The deployed probability read is now `gaussian_median`, which the script did not know; the pick-space shift for it is the residual median (measured 0.369057 on 886 residuals, and `sign(residual + 0.369057)` reproduces all 16 served sides). (3) Its argparse description read `__doc__`, which the docstring ban empties. Regenerated as `artifacts/waterfall_feed/20260911T034354Z` stamped `active_model_id d49194e04945a5e5` and `feature_table_sha256 2fb3451b...`, 16 games. The guard that stops this silently recurring: `board_content.require_active_waterfall_feed` raises `StaleWaterfallFeedError` (a `ValueError`, so the CLI exits 2 with a plain line) when the feed's model id or feature digest differs from the active model, checked once where the feed is loaded and again inside `_build_attribution`; aimed at the old feed it prints "The per-game attribution feed was built for model '3083f6cbc5e45acb', but the active model is 'd49194e04945a5e5'. Rebuild it before publishing: .\.tools\uv.exe run --no-sync python scripts/waterfall_feed.py" and writes no pages. Regenerate command, to run after each Tuesday publish and after any refresh that changes the forecast: `.\.tools\uv.exe run --no-sync python scripts/waterfall_feed.py`. `publish-board` re-run clean: index.html 3,189,793 bytes, 354 tokens added / 370 removed of 269,385 -- the 16 attribution panels and the publish stamp; the home-side correction row now appears on exactly the two big-spread games. No new tests (moratorium); gates 4,529 passed / 9 skipped, `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, `mypy src` clean. **2026-09-11 (UI-20 lane): the weak-spots table gains a time-of-season row, computed from the active model's own opener evaluation at render time, never from a doc.** MOD-06's screen (b) found production's own served opener picks going 56.01% in weeks 1-9 against 53.05% in weeks 10 and later, with the stated confidence flat at 0.557/0.558/0.558 across the season -- exactly the weak-spots material the Model page exists to surface, and the doc said so explicitly. New `nfl_ats.model_weak_spots.build_season_timing` (separate from `build_weak_spots`, so the existing fixture that omits `season`/`week` columns is untouched) splits the opener archive into weeks 1-9 and weeks 10-18, computes accuracy and stated confidence for each half, and reports the early-minus-late gap on both -- all with the same `nfl_ats.clv.week_blocked_bootstrap` resampled-range treatment the MOD-06 doc itself used (weeks as blocks, a percentile interval, `probability_positive_from_draws` rendered as "N% likely real" to match the existing `_humanize_probability_positive` convention). Wired into `board_site_content.load_model_weak_spots` beside the existing home-correction load, and rendered as a new "Early season vs. late season" subsection on `model.html`'s weak-spots table (`board_terminal.py`), guarded by the same active-model match the rest of the table already uses. Measured live off the active model's own archive (`artifacts/opener_evaluation/20260910T211255Z`, matches the doc exactly): weeks 1-9 766 games, 56.0% right, resampled range [52.4%, 59.6%], stated confidence 55.9%; weeks 10-18 737 games, 53.1% right, resampled range [49.3%, 56.7%], stated confidence 55.6%; early minus late +3.0 points, resampled range [-2.1%, +8.3%] (87% likely real), while stated confidence moved only +0.3 points over the same split, resampled range [-0.3%, +0.9%] (82% likely real) -- the mismatch MOD-06 flagged, now visible on the page itself rather than only in a doc. `publish-board` re-run clean (all four pages); the new fragment on `model.html` is 1,380 bytes, isolated by extracting the `weak-spots-season-h` block directly since the file's other lanes' concurrent edits make a whole-file byte diff a mixed signal. `tests/test_model_weak_spots.py` (10 tests, unmodified) and `tests/test_board_humanised.py::test_model_page_is_humanised` (real-artifact render-contract scan for ids/hashes/timestamps/`P+`/`week-blocked`/snake_case) both green against the live render; no new test files or functions (moratorium). `ruff format`, `ruff check`, `mypy src` clean on `model_weak_spots.py`, `board_site_content.py`, `board_terminal.py`. **2026-09-11 (lane 1, verified live by the coordinator on the regenerated page):** candidates (a) per-pick 'Why this pick' and (f) the injury-report banner were already shipped. Found and fixed a live contradiction: all 16 'Why this pick' panels read 'injuries was a bit stale' while the banner said reports were fully in, because `card_explanation._freshness_clause` lumped the permanently not-configured Sportradar source into the injury freshness bucket; `not_configured`/`not_due` are now neutral (matching the SOURCES legend), `scripts/regenerate_pick_explanations.py` re-rendered the current week's text from its stored components (16 changed, no probability touched). **Coordinator, same day:** the picks table now shows the side actually being played — the latest recorded mid-week change per game (DAL at NYG -> NYG, WAS at PHI -> PHI this week), with the decision score recomputed for that side and floored at 50% — so the 'picks table has not caught up' footnote no longer fires; and the tracked card's late-week section now lists every game whose played side differs from the published table (3 this week), not only this pass's delta. **2026-09-11 (lane 2, (b)/(h)):** (h) the History page's opener-vs-close per-season and per-week tables were already shipped (2026-09-05); 2026 Week 1 shows 'No close line archived for this week' because no close quote is captured yet, a capture gap not a render defect. (b) 'Research this week' on findings.html was live but out of spec: it dumped all 5,119 matching registry entries into ten category blocks (one held 4,181 rows), pushing the page past 2.3 MB, and the top entries were disguised instrument checks (positive controls, perfect-foresight ceilings). New `findings_registry._is_instrument_control_text` flags those; the section is now a flat list capped at eight one-line findings ('… chance it helps: NN%'), header states looked at / resolved / still open (5,338 / 37 / 5,301 measured); findings.html is 345 KB. Three tests in `test_public_board_wave1.py` edited to the new contract, none added. **2026-09-11 (lane 3, Rival rules panel; rendered text verified by the coordinator):** the This Week panel read 'None are recorded for this week yet' while the challenger ledger held 600 Week 1 rows across 41 rules. Root cause: `board_content._build_rival_rules` filtered the paper ledger to rows whose policy id equalled the live constant; Week 1's rows were recorded under the prior policy id two hours before that session bumped the served policy, so the filter emptied the played card every time — the same bug fixed on 2026-09-10 in `_build_prospective_scoreboard` and missed here. Filter removed; the panel now summarises how many whole-card rules disagree and where (31 pick a whole card, 29 disagree somewhere, BAL at IND most contested at 26 of 31), lists only the disagreeing rules with the team each takes, and shows each rule's record on its disagreeing games beside the card's own record there once settled (8 rules already behind the card, the same 8 History's season-so-far block names from the same ledger). Also found: `nfl-ats settle --write-graded` had not run since the two games went final, so every graded ledger still read NaN; re-running it (104 settled / 903 pending) also cleared the served-refresh-chain model-id mismatch that had publish-board failing closed. **2026-09-12 (Saturday):** the headline "played card" archive number had vanished from the board (`archive score not recomputed for this model`): the number is read from `artifacts/unserved_tilt_marginals`, whose runner `scripts/unserved_tilt_marginals.py` was deleted in the b7ed31d repo cut, so the Friday 12:09 refit (model `7786467eabe418b8`) left nothing keyed to the active model. Runner restored, wrapped as `nfl-ats served-card-archive` (reuse-or-measure, same shape as `served-refresh-card`) and wired into weekly-run step 7 so a refit can no longer blank the number; re-measured at 56.75% on 1,503 games. Second reader-facing change: the Injury reports line now states, from the latest league feed copy, how many game-status designations are actually in hand for the teams still to play, when the newest entries arrived and when the feed was last checked -- because on Saturday morning the feed carried 8 designations, all for the Wednesday/Thursday games, while Friday's designations for the Sunday slate existed only in the news (see LEAD-64). Report times on the board are now Eastern, not UTC. **2026-09-12 afternoon (owner asks, all shipped):** (1) a Books now column beside the pool line: the median line across the books in the latest capture, written for the picked side, with the move since the pool line in the hover (PIT -6 against a pool -3.5); (2) Best Pick confidence on the rail and a note under the table saying how it is chosen (highest model chance among the games where the books agreed most closely on the line, re-nominated only Sunday morning); (3) an explicit Quarterback sentence at the top of Why this pick, and a QB1 out tag in the Pick column, whenever a listed starter is Out or Doubtful: who is out, whether the listed backup is out too, the start chance the model priced, and how many points the model's quarterback inputs move the game and toward whom (from the attribution feed); (4) the lineup panel now lists players in projected order (a ruled-out player sinks below the available ones, its listed slot shown in parentheses) instead of depth-chart order that showed Tua at QB1 after he was ruled Out; (5) the Flips at column shows one number in the adverse direction only, or 'holds through' the adverse sweep edge -- the old 'holds from +0.5 to -7.5' wording and the flip search in the favourable direction are gone (owner directive, see AGENTS.md). **Correction the same afternoon (owner: item 2 missed the mark):** the reader wants the Best Pick ORDER, not a confidence number. Every refresh pass now writes `artifacts/best_pick_ranking/latest.json` (the re-nomination table: every game still to play, the played side's model cover chance, the spread's spread across books, eligible or not) and the board renders it under the table as a numbered list -- 1. MIA +3.5 58.6%, 2. DET -6.5 55.7%, 3. CIN -3.5 50.8% ... with the games the books disagree on listed after, unranked -- stamped with the pass time. |

## Phase 11 — ambitious research

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| SKY-01 | 🌙 | News-to-availability model | Time-stamped reports mapped to player status with source provenance |
| SKY-02 | 🌙 | Video-derived tracking proxies | Only with lawful data and reproducible computer-vision evaluation |
| SKY-03 | 🌙 | Causal matchup effects | Separate persistent skill from opponent selection and game script |
| SKY-04 | 🌙 | Market microstructure model | Quote arrivals, book leadership, latency, and information diffusion. **2026-09-03 book-leadership descriptive measured** (`docs/book_leadership.md`, frozen design; `scripts/book_leadership.py`, `tests/test_book_leadership.py`, artifact `artifacts/book_leadership/20260903T201202Z/`): 855 games / 7,424 snapshots / 27,548 move events (2023–2025 spreads) — Bovada 5,044 first-move credits, williamhill_us highest share 0.64, big US retail books follow at 0.30–0.45; staleness medians 35–100s. Caveats disclosed (provider-clock trust, coarse cadence, first-is-not-right). Descriptive only: no registry entry, no window. The full model (latency, diffusion, sharpness) remains a moonshot. |
| SKY-05 | 🌙 | Automated research agent | Propose experiments but require fixed budgets and human approval |
| SKY-06 | ✅ | Public forecast archive | **Tamper-evident foundation implemented; signing remains open** (`docs/public_forecast_archive.md`, 2026-09-02). A production-isolated JSONL library now canonicalizes probability records sufficient for later calibration, pins model/configuration/feature/prediction provenance, requires `inputs_observed_through <= decision <= publication < kickoff`, appends under an exclusive lock, and chains deterministic SHA-256 content hashes through `previous_record_sha256`. Verification recomputes canonical bytes, schema/chronology, every content hash and link, with an optional separately pinned head; focused tests cover append-only prefix preservation, tampering, broken links, backdating, and the standalone `python -m nfl_ats.public_forecast_archive verify` command. **Closed 2026-09-03 by owner decision:** cryptographic signing (non-repudiation of authorship) is overkill for a personal research project with no adversary — the hash chain plus git history plus Pages deploy log is the practical tamper evidence, and it is already built, tested, and verified. Reopen only if the benchmark goes public and needs third-party identity proof. No secret, live archive, publisher/card/ledger integration, model, or registry decision changed. |
| SKY-07 | 🚧 | Open benchmark suite | **Deterministic offline foundation implemented** (`src/nfl_ats/open_benchmark.py`, `docs/open_benchmark_suite.md`, 2026-09-02): canonical CSV/JSON exports pin schema, source URLs, SPDX license, hashes, row counts, chronological split boundaries, point-in-time timestamps, and withheld test labels. Submission exports are deterministic, complete, unscored, and pinned to the exact dataset manifest (11 contract tests pass). **2026-09-03 input scoping (blocker 1 resolved as scope):** per tracked `config/source_policies.json`, the only redistribution-clean input is nflverse (raw + derived `allowed_with_attribution`, CC-BY-4.0) — so a publishable release is game identity/kickoff/spread/scores plus in-repo features computed solely from nflverse inputs, with per-dataset upstream license checks still owed (some nflverse datasets carry different terms). Excluded by policy: Odds API raw quotes, weather vendors, Sagarin, PFR, Sportradar, CFBD (aggregates-only or review-gated). Remaining owner decisions: license posting + source URLs, hosting, test-label custody, leaderboard metrics. No model comparison was run. |

## Phase 12 — open lead queue

Sixty unmeasured proposals added 2026-09-04 against the 85-row idea ledger
(`docs/archive/idea_ledger.md`) — every one below is cut to avoid duplicating
it. Nothing here has been scored, no registry window has been spent, and no
number below is a finding: each row states its mechanism, its PREDECLARED
direction (the family is declared before any sign is seen, per AGENTS.md),
and the instrument that decides it. Default routing: screen on the free
CFB XLG-03 benchmark where the mechanism exists in college; NFL ATS looks
go through `rotation declare` for a fresh family window and are graded at
the opener; anything playable without touching outcomes (source builds,
dashboard, simulations) is marked ⬜ and may start immediately. Whatever
gets measured is recorded through both registries with
`probability_positive`, never "contains zero".

| ID | Status | Item | Definition of done |
|---|---|---|---|
| LEAD-01 | ✅ | Wednesday-revision follow | Splash revises Tuesday lines Wednesday; the revision carries 24h of news and flow the frozen grade cannot contain. Predeclared direction: FOLLOW the revision on Tue→Wed moves ≥1.0 pt (recomputed pick graded at the frozen Tuesday line, POL-11 machinery). Instrument: 2023–2025 hourly archive, Wed-noon minus Tue-open. | LEAD-08 | ✅ | Friday designation momentum | DNP→LP (healing trajectory) vs LP→DNP (deteriorating) vs static Friday labels predict Sunday status beyond the label itself. Instrument: NFL.com 2022–24 Wed/Thu/Fri revision depth; distinct from the static OUT-count screen (trajectory, not level). **2026-09-05 CX14 measured: recorded unresolved refresh screen on 1,047 production-paired opener games; 0 flips, +0.000 accuracy points, probability_positive=0.00000, probability_tie=1; zero construct coverage, no directional mechanism evidence; inactive oracle unavailable; docs/injury_trajectory_leads.md.** **2026-09-11 measured (LEAD-08 lane):** confirmed a third way that the literal Wed/Thu/Fri per-player trajectory has zero coverage in every ingested source — the canonical `data/raw/nflverse_injuries/20260910T203021Z/injuries.parquet` (76,784-class PER-03 history) stores exactly one row per player-week (90,887/90,889 season/week/team/gsis_id groups have count 1), so no player ever has both an early-week and a Friday observation to sequence; this is a data-availability fact, not a mechanism finding, and not a closing ground. Built the closest available proxy instead — a revision-timing class (`static`: date_modified's Eastern weekday never moves past Wed; `revised_late`: it does) — and ran the full pipeline on it. Base rates (76,554 covered player-weeks, 2010–2024): `revised_late` inactive (snap-count-proxy) rate 46.886% (n=70,123) vs `static` 45.685% (n=6,431), same-direction gap at every practice level (DNP +14.30pp, LP +5.09pp, FP +2.52pp). Sign is not uniform across seasons (2020 +0.45 P+0.71, 2021 −1.27 P+0.12, 2022 +1.61 P+0.82), so `wrong_sign_resolved` is inadmissible; reliability is positive, so `no_split_half_reliability` is inadmissible. | LEAD-18 | ✅ | Concussion recovery by position | Skill-position Wednesday-concussion DNPs sit more often than line-concussion DNPs (recovery curves differ). Predeclared direction: positional split predicts Sunday action (feeds the availability model; QUALITY, no ATS claim). 2022–24 text rows. **2026-09-05 measured, unresolved** (`docs/injury_report_hygiene.md`, `src/nfl_ats/injury_report_hygiene.py`, `scripts/injury_report_hygiene_screen.py`): population widened to 2013-2024 (task-specified 2013-2025; the snapshot's own manifest discloses `date_modified` is 100% null for 2025, so `nfl_ats.players.canonicalize_injuries` — production's own injury canonicalizer — already drops that season too; not a new gap). Concussion+DNP player-weeks sit out Sunday 99.13% of the time REGARDLESS of position (1,254 of 1,265) — a ceiling effect leaving little room for a positional split. Skill-minus-line sit-rate gap, season-blocked bootstrap (2,000 draws, seed 20260905): 2013-2024 **-0.00328, 95% [-0.01674, +0.00849], P+ 0.3365** (n=452 skill/359 line); by era 2013-2017 **+0.00701, P+ 0.7765**; 2018-2024 **-0.01145, 95% [-0.02536, 0.00000], P+ 0.0000** (line rate is a literal 1.0, 196/196, mechanically flooring the upper tail — CI touches but does not clear zero, so `wrong_sign_resolved` does not apply per AGENTS.md). Predeclared direction not supported by this population; comparison population (non-concussion DNP) DOES show a robust, whole-interval-positive skill-vs-line gap both eras (2013-2024: **+0.09612, 95% [+0.07513,+0.11674], P+ 1.0**), so the generic position-group split stays justified, it just does not extend to concussions. No `nfl_ats.weak_signals.EFFECT_UNITS` value is commensurable with a raw sit-rate gap (rate difference, not an ATS/forecast metric); per AGENTS.md's commensurability rule, NOT recorded in the weak-signals registry — this row, `docs/injury_report_hygiene.md`, and `artifacts/injury_report_hygiene/20260905T044503Z/results.json` are the record. |
| LEAD-19 | ✅ | Personal-matter DNP exclusion audit | "Personal reasons" DNPs resolve unpredictably; measure their Sunday-action base rate to justify EXCLUDING them from availability features. No ATS direction (feature-hygiene measurement, valuable either way). **2026-09-05 measured, decision: keep-with-separate-flag** (`docs/injury_report_hygiene.md`, same code as LEAD-18; population 2013-2024, see that row's note on the disclosed 2025 `date_modified` gap): frozen exact-match designation vocabulary (5 disjoint buckets, full list in the doc) applied to 17,508 DNP player-weeks. Personal-matter DNPs play the following Sunday **53.3%** of the time (n=227, all 2018-2024 — zero personal-matter rows exist 2013-2017), far above genuine-injury DNPs at the same practice status (**7.9%**, n=14,752; gap **+0.454, 95% [+0.265,+0.605], P+ 1.0**) and far below rest-day DNPs (**95.7%**, n=897; gap **-0.423, 95% [-0.635,-0.253], P+ 0.0**) — personal-matter is statistically distinct from both categories a feature designer might fold it into. Decision implied: neither exclude (53.3% is informative) nor merge into either neighboring bucket (would bias both away from their true rates); the specific mixed cell this risk is concrete for (`report_status` blank + DNP practice status, the combo `nfl_ats.availability`'s learned-rate table already keys on) mixes played rates 30.3%-96.7% across designations within one pooled cell today (766 genuine-injury rows are only 28.7% of that 2,672-row cell). Same commensurability conclusion as LEAD-18: no `EFFECT_UNITS` value fits a rate difference, NOT recorded in the weak-signals registry; doc + `artifacts/injury_report_hygiene/20260905T044503Z/results.json` are the record. Valuable either way, per this row's own predeclaration: the audit answers the exclude/keep/flag question regardless of which way the numbers came out. |

The frozen Tuesday `is_best_pick` on the paper-decision ledger is never rewritten. **One deliberate deviation from the historical arm, stated plainly:** the statistic is the cover probability of the side the pass SERVES, not of the Tuesday side -- identical on every game whose pick has not moved, and the only coherent choice once the late-week follow has flipped one (measured 2026-09-10 on Week 1: the Tuesday-side ranking would have starred WAS at PHI at 0.632 on a game whose served pick is PHI, whose own probability is 0.368); both numbers are reported on every pass. The live challenger `best_pick_sunday_renomination` was implementing S1 and now ranks with the served S3 inputs. | LEAD-63 | ✅ | In-game QB exit as a pregame mixture | Owner, 2026-09-10, after the Week 1 opener: the Seattle starter left in the first minutes and Seattle won by 3, half a point short of covering; the pick was right, but the owner asked whether the pick should be a mixture over "starter finishes" and "starter exits", weighted by a pregame exit probability, rather than a single starter-plays-all-game forecast. Today's model carries only pregame QB status (QB-continuity features, the backup-QB fade and injury-value overlays); nothing conditions on an in-game exit. Build in three steps, all on data already on disk. (1) Label starter early exits 2009-2025 from play-by-play: starter's last dropback share of the game, flag exits before the fourth quarter; report the base rate per team-game and by season. (2) Model the pregame exit probability from what is visible at the deadline: injury-report status of the starter (questionable / limited / DNP that week), age, prior-season games missed, sack rate faced; report split-half reliability of the flag. (3) Score the mixture pick: cover probability = P(finishes) x P(cover | finishes) + P(exits) x P(cover | exits), where P(cover | exits) comes from the existing backup-QB construct; grade forced-pick accuracy against production at the opener on the assigned window, report `probability_positive`, and record through `nfl-ats weak-signals record`. Expectation stated before seeing signs: the base rate is a few percent and symmetric, so the mixture moves only picks already near 50%; the value, if any, is in the asymmetric cell (starter on the report but active). An interval containing zero is not grounds to close this; only a resolved wrong sign, zero split-half reliability of the exit flag, or a positive-control bound is. **2026-09-10 (measured):** all three steps run; window `qb_exit_mixture_on_production` assigned and spent on the 2020-2021 opener block. (1) Labels 2009-2025 from pbp `20260817T184927Z` into `data/processed/qb_starter_exits.parquet`: base rate **3.804% of team-games** (337 of 8,860 REG), per season **2.539% (2018) to 5.859% (2010)**, rising since 2018 (2025 = 4.779%); starter share 95.98%; relief QB takes **59.69%** of dropbacks when a starter does leave. (2) Split-half reliability of the flag, odd vs even seasons: **0.4868** per starting QB (93 QBs, r = 0.3217 [0.1109, 0.5276]) and **0.6873** per team (32 teams) -- far above the 0.10 floor, so `no_split_half_reliability` is unavailable to this lane forever. Pregame logistic on injury status / experience / prior missed starts / opponent sack rate, trained only on earlier seasons: AUC **0.6426** and Brier skill **+0.0021** over 2013-2025, AUC **0.5757** on the window; mean predicted 3.32% against 3.22% observed. (3) Mixture screen at d = -0.029095 (read from `bias_battery_backup_qb_start_opener`'s raw_gap): **-0.21978 accuracy points**, week-blocked 95% **[-0.90703, +0.44944]**, **`probability_positive` = 0.29240**, 52.9670% vs production 53.1868% on 455 non-push games / 35 weeks, **3 picks changed**. Asymmetric cell (exactly one starter on the injury report and active) is **129 games, 0 picks changed, 0.00000 points**. **Measured cause:** the mixture's mean absolute probability shift is **0.000554** (max 0.003546) against production probabilities 0.0683 wide -- d is the whole story. Controls: oracle exit flag at the same d moves 2 picks and 0.00000 points but improves **Brier by +0.000335 at `probability_positive` 0.86500**, i.e. the direction is right and the magnitude cannot reach the pick threshold; oracle at d = -0.50 moves 11 picks for +0.21978 points, itself unresolved at `probability_positive` 0.63103, so it supplies **no** `positive_control_bound`. Seven signals recorded under family `qb_exit_mixture` (`unresolved_below_power`), rotation verdict `unresolved`. **Not closed** -- three named follow-ups in `docs/qb_exit_mixture.md`: measure an in-game-handover-specific d (snap share, relief QB quality, injury vs benching); strengthen the exit probability, which is thin on 2020-2021 and gets nothing from opponent sack rate; and apply the mixture to the served probability and the discrete key-number margin lattice rather than only to the pick side, where the oracle control's Brier gain appeared. Script `scripts/qb_exit_mixture_screen.py`, artifact `artifacts/experiments/qb_exit_mixture/results.json`. | Done 2026-09-10: screen `unresolved_below_power`, window spent, three follow-ups named in the doc; no card change. |

The headline parser stays queued as the same-day fallback for a morning the release is late. **Lineup panel defect found 12:30 ET the same day:** the This Week lineup panel (`scripts/build_week_lineups.py`) fetched the live nflverse release and stamped every 2026 row with the week_proxy fallback (kickoff minus 24 h), so on Saturday it saw 29 of 182 report rows and showed ATL QB1 Tua Tagovailoa as "no report / plays 99%" after he had been ruled Out. It now reads the player snapshot (rows stamped at the capture that first saw them, 182/182 visible) and carries each player's designation onto the panel (Tua: Out, 0%). Remaining limit: with both ATL quarterbacks Out the play-probability model gives QB3 Cooper Rush 19% to play / 4% to start, because it has no two-quarterbacks-out history to learn from; the panel is context only, the pick does not read it. |
| ENG-43 | ✅ | Odds captures around the Friday designations | Measured 2026-09-12: no odds capture existed between odds_thu_tnf (Thu 18:00 ET) and odds_sat (Sat 12:00 ET); ATL at PIT moved -3.5 -> -4.5/-5.5 (Thu 18:00) -> -5.5/-6 (Sat 09:40) in that hole, and Friday's 13:30 last call and Saturday's 07:29 pass both re-decided on Thursday's lines. Added odds_fri_1230, odds_fri_1800 and odds_sat_1000 (each just ahead of a refresh pass that reads it; 3 quota credits per capture, 98,900 remaining), exercised all three, daemon restarted on the new schedule (pid 28736). A refresh pass on the fresh lines followed. |
| ENG-44 | ✅ | Refresh passes took 17-48 minutes instead of 8 | Measured 2026-09-12 with py-spy on a live pass: a two-minute sample was 100% inside the card-ledger consistency check and 80% of that inside `fetch_mos_bulletin` -- every card resolution re-fetched every game's weather bulletins from the Iowa mesonet MOS endpoint (0.3 s each plus a 0.3 s sleep, walking back up to ten issuance times when the newest are not yet published), and a recording pass resolves the card several times (ledger check, publish-card, the weather challenger recorders). Second cost: `load_quote_history` concatenated all 8,805 market snapshots (8,746 of them the 2020-2025 historical backfill, 6.2M rows, 37 s per call) for a current-week read, and the pass called it three times. Fixes: bulletins are cached on disk under data/raw/forecast_archive/mos_bulletins (a bulletin for a given station, model and issuance time never changes; an empty answer is final 36 h after the issuance time and re-checked every 30 min before that) plus an in-process memo; `load_quote_history` takes `since=` and the refresh-path readers (current line, refresh-day dispersion, Tuesday-opener dispersion fallback) pass a 2-21 day floor. Non-recording pass: 7 min before, see the session report for after; the served 12:54 ET last-call pass with a warm cache took 6 min. **Third and largest cause, found by counting the HTTP calls (measured 13:20 ET):** the kickoff-nearest fetch asked the mesonet for the issuance nearest the pool deadline -- for every Sunday game that is Sunday 12Z, which does not exist yet on Saturday -- got a 404, treated it as a transport failure, retried three times with sleeps, and gave up on the game: 105 s and 42 calls per card resolution, and NO kickoff-time weather for any Sunday game on any Saturday pass all week (the cold-visitor, precipitation and warm-team rules were running fail-open on zero flags). Fixed: issuance times later than now are skipped, a 404 is an empty bulletin (cached), so the read falls back to the newest bulletin that exists (Saturday 12Z GFS for all 15 mappable games, 14 s cold, 9 s warm). A served pass was run on the fix. |

**Historical pointer refreshed 2026-09-01 (afternoon fleet session; every number below is measured that day from the named artifact or registry entry).** Every item the 2026-08-31 pointer named as pending has now run or been resolved:

1. **The graph-on-production line is three-for-three negative, and the reliability premise is what failed.** `graph_team_stat_off_rush_epa_per_play` measured on top of PRODUCTION `weak_stack` (close-graded, rotation-assigned [2014, 2016], 749 games, 51 weeks; `docs/graph_team_stat_off_rush_epa_on_production.md`, artifact `artifacts/graph_team_stat_off_rush_epa_per_play_on_production/20260901T184239Z/results.json`): paired delta **-0.935 accuracy points**, week-blocked 95% **[-1.998, +0.135]**, `probability_positive` **0.037**; candidate 50.07% vs production 51.00%; observed at the 0.5th percentile of its own within-week null. Recorded `unresolved_below_power` under `graph_off_rush_epa_on_production` and the window spent `unresolved`; no card change. With `off_sack_rate` (-0.935, P+ 0.122) and `def_yards_per_play` (-0.668, P+ 0.189) all three cells the 38-family screen carried forward now read negative on production. The family's highest split-half reliability (0.987), replicated in sign on a disjoint opener-graded window (+1.996 pts, P+ 0.828), did **not** predict survival on top of production: reliability measures consistency, not whether the played chain already prices the trait. All three graph on-production families report `remaining_eligible_windows: 0`.
2. **The CFB replication explains the shape of that result.** On the XLG-03 clean core (8,933 graded games, 199 weeks; `docs/graph_team_stat_cfb_replication.md`), the graph column BEATS the raw differential as a single feature on all three available cells (+0.369 / +0.291 / +0.694 accuracy points, week-blocked P+ 0.798 / 0.765 / 0.897, moving 17-26% of picks) -- the first cross-league corroboration of the `graph_input_screen` family -- but ADDED on top of a benchmark that already carries the raw statistic it is worth about nothing (-0.011 / +0.022 / -0.179 points, P+ 0.467 / 0.535 / 0.266, intervals of +/-0.29 to +/-0.69 points, roughly four times tighter than the NFL on-production reading's). Interpretive consequence: the NFL -0.935 / -0.668 readings sit inside the noise of a true effect near zero and should be described as consistent with a null, not as the graph damaging the chain. The graph lane, if pursued, is a REPLACEMENT for a raw team-state input, not another column (queued as WP24 on CFB first). `off_sack_rate` has no CFB counterpart (no sack/pressure column in the CFB table).
3. **`fluview_home_market_elevated` now has three independent negative-leaning reads against one close-graded positive.** The family's second opener window, (2022, 2023), read **-1.751 pts, 95% [-4.501, +0.986], P+ 0.094** (`docs/fluview_opener_look.md` sections 8-9; first window -0.439, P+ 0.341; a doc-only post-hoc union read 2020-2023 -1.134, P+ 0.113), and the CFB replication reads -0.388, P+ 0.200 on 5,671 games (XLG-08). Nothing closes (no interval wholly on one side of zero; trait reliability 0.98 in both leagues), but spending further NFL window on this cell is now a worse bet than it looked; the family has no default-size opener block left.
